# Web quality and device audit — Haiyo web app

Date: 2026-11-24 (sources read 2026-11-23). Code audited: branch `claude/agent-family-review-8a6084` at `2567383` (includes this week's phone-layout work), `app/web` plus the parts of `app/service` that serve it; the iOS shell read from `origin/lane/mac-app-shell` at `585dcaa` (`app/ios`, read with `git show`). Read-only: nothing in the repository was changed except this file. No app run, no test suite run, no installs.

How this was done: the guidance repository was downloaded at one commit and all 252 guides were read in full by ten reviewers, one slice each, who checked our code against every guide and cited the file and line. I merged their tables, checked a sample of the citations against the code, and did Part 2 (devices) myself. The iPhone Duo observations marked **measured** were made on the iPhone Duo simulator on our Mac (iOS 27.2, the current shell build) and passed to me; nothing here was run on a real device.

---

## Summary

**Part 1 in numbers.** 252 guides, checked as 547 rows (broad "hub" guides are split into one row per rule). Rows: **Meets 116 · Partly 209 · Does not meet 74 · Not applicable 148**. Per guide (a guide counts as "meets" only when every rule of it that applies meets): **Meets 21 · Partly 92 · Does not meet 25 · Not applicable 114**. Of the 283 rows not met in full: 6 High, 88 Medium, 189 Low.

Strong already: the Markdown pipeline and link rules (allow-list, no `innerHTML`), strict origin checks and CSP, no third-party origins, dynamic viewport units, `viewport-fit=cover` with insets on the main layers, reduced-motion handling, hover styles fenced behind `(hover: hover)`, 16 px fields on touch screens, no user-agent sniffing.

**The ten findings that matter most**

| # | Severity | Finding | Where in this report |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | High | There is no in-app account deletion; App Store guideline 5.1.1(v) requires it before the iOS app can be submitted (planned as S7, scheduled last). | 1.8 privacy §7 |
| 2 | High | iPad is not supported: the shell is iPhone-only (`TARGETED_DEVICE_FAMILY = 1`, portrait only), so iPad — and App Review — gets compatibility mode, where the iPadOS window controls sit over the page's top-left back button (measured); and at real iPad widths the web layout shows portrait iPads (744–834 pt) one pane at a time beside a 60 px rail. | 2.3, 2.7 |
| 3 | High | Back (Android back, the iOS edge swipe the shell turns on) with a dialog open closes the dialog **and** navigates the page under it; dialogs are `div`s, so the page behind is not inert and not covered by the top layer. | 1.12 platform-controls-dismiss-dialog; 1.13 dialog |
| 4 | High | On one-column widths every open and every Back drops keyboard and screen-reader focus to `<body>`; `document.title` is "Haiyo" on every view. | 1.13 stack-drill-down; 1.1 §page-titles |
| 5 | High | The muted text colour `--ink-3` is 3.3–4.5:1 (needs 4.5:1) in about 120 places (times, hints, labels); the unread badge is 3.9:1 and the solid tag 3.4:1. | 1.15 color §text-contrast |
| 6 | High | The toast's live region is mounted together with its text, so VoiceOver may not announce it, yet some errors exist only there; it also sits over the composer and catches taps meant for it. | 1.13 persistent-toast-notifications |
| 7 | Medium | Components size themselves by the window, never by their pane (no container queries): the chat pane is as narrow as ~430 px at an 861 px window and ~450–520 pt on the Duo's inner display, where file cards squeeze (measured: a name cut to "sd1…0991_2.mp4", 「仅本会话可见」 wrapping). | 1.3 size-aware-styling; 2.3 |
| 8 | Medium | iPhone Duo half-open: the fold (x ≈ 476 pt) runs through the chat pane 44 pt from its left edge — header avatar, incoming avatars, the first composer button; nothing avoids it (measured), and a page in a WKWebView cannot detect the fold, so the shell must pass it in. | 2.1, 2.3 |
| 9 | Medium | Safe areas are assumed symmetric or ignored by several fixed layers — the toast centred on the full width, full-bleed phone picker sheets, the details panel's left edge, JS-placed popovers, picture widths from `100vw`, and the shell's offline screen — which on the Duo's outer display reach under the right-hand status column. | 2.4 |
| 10 | Medium | Esc in a dialog (including the Esc that cancels a Pinyin candidate) closes it and loses what was typed; Enter in the provider form's model-name box ignores IME composition and adds a half-typed name. | 1.12 light-dismiss-a-dialog, platform-controls-dismiss-dialog §esc; 1.13 dialog §dirty-form-guard; 1.4 ime-safe-enter-submit |

Also notable (all Medium): text ignores the OS text size (14 px root, no `rem`; Dynamic Type has no effect in the shell); no height awareness (the rail needs ~454 px, so landscape phones lose 设置 — computed); every streamed delta rebuilds the whole view model and re-renders every subscriber; chat pictures are full originals and the 676 KB logo PNG doubles as a 38 px mark and the favicon; no dark mode (the shell forces Light); faint input borders and focus rings; no passkeys.

**The proposed layout rule (2.6), in two sentences.** Classify the space actually available to the page — viewport minus safe-area insets — on each axis: width is *regular* from 720 px and *compact* below, height is *regular* from 500 px and *compact* below; regular width shows the list and the conversation side by side (the details panel docks as a third column only from 1280 px), compact width shows one pane at a time, and navigation is a bottom tab bar only when width is compact and height regular, a side rail otherwise. Everything inside a pane adapts to that pane's width through container queries, never to the window, and when a fold is reported the side-by-side split moves to the fold.

---

## Part 1 — Modern Web Guidance

### About the guidance

- **What it is.** The source repository of Chrome's "Modern Web Guidance": https://github.com/GoogleChrome/modern-web-guidance-src (read 2026-11-23 at commit `920ee542`, pushed 2026-11-22). It is a library of guides that coding agents retrieve through a CLI/skill (`npx modern-web-guidance search` / `retrieve`). The README says it is stewarded by the Chrome, Edge, Firefox and MDN teams, targets Baseline "widely available" features, and requires a fallback or progressive enhancement for newer ones; single-engine features stay "volatile" (opt-in) for three months, origin-trial features are opt-in.
- **How it is organised.** `guides/<category>/<use-case>/`, each with `guide.md` (what an agent reads), `demo.html` (a reference implementation) and `expectations.md` (testable requirements used to build graders). 17 categories: accessibility, built-in-ai, css, forms, html, js, performance, privacy, pwa, security, ui-atoms, ui-behaviors, ui-components, user-experience, visual-design, wasm, webmcp. Some guides are broad "discipline hubs" (accessibility, css, html, js, performance, security, privacy, forms, color, typography, motion…); the rest are single use cases. The remainder of the repository is the CLI, the evaluation harness and a results dashboard.
- **What was read.** Every `guide.md` (252 files, about 1.9 MB), the README, `guides/README.md`, `guides/CONTRIBUTING.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/LEXICON.md`, the skill entry `guides/modern-web-guidance/SKILL.md`, the `features/*.md` notes and the licence. Not read: `demo.html`, `expectations.md`, task and harness files (test material, not guidance). Nothing failed to download.
- **Licence.** Code: Apache License 2.0. Guide content under `guides/`: Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 (CC BY 4.0), with portions derived from MDN and W3C/WHATWG/IETF specifications. This report takes ideas only and paraphrases; it copies no guide text beyond a few words.
- **Guides with no body yet.** 34 guides are frontmatter only (a name, a one-line description, feature ids) and were judged on their description: css/css-conditionals; forms/combobox, cycle-toggle, filterable-select, rating, slider-range, slider-tooltip, tree-view; html/camera-and-microphone-capture, html-markers, user-location; pwa/pwa, app-badging-and-notifications, background-sync-and-transfers, custom-window-title-bar, installable-web-app-metadata, offline-loading-and-updates, os-content-sharing, os-launch-and-file-handling; ui-behaviors/client-side-routing, hierarchical-navigation, line-drawing-animation, overflow-popup, responsive-disclosure; ui-components/checkbox-group, content-table, data-grid, media-player-controls, skeleton; user-experience/guided-navigation, preference-aware-ui; visual-design/color-ramps, element-shape, layout-dividers. Eight more are marked draft in the guidance itself (stub: css/masonry-layout, ui-atoms/custom-list-markers, visual-design/fit-text-to-container, visual-design/gradient-borders; blocked: forms/button-group and three Cross-Origin-Storage performance guides).

### How to read the tables

- One table per guidance category. "Guide § rule" is the guide id (the category prefix is sometimes dropped) and, for hub guides, the rule. "What the guidance asks" is our paraphrase.
- Verdict: **Meets**, **Partly**, **Does not meet**, **N/A** (the situation does not arise in our app; the reason is in the row).
- Severity, only for Partly / Does not meet: **High** = users hit it (broken, inaccessible, insecure, or wrong on a real device); **Medium** = a noticeable quality or performance gap; **Low** = polish or modernisation.
- Evidence: paths starting `app/` are from the repository root; short paths such as `styles.css:63`, `pages/…`, `ui/…`, `data/…`, `auth/…` are under `app/web/src/`.
- Our browser floor is Chrome/Edge 111, Firefox 114, Safari/iOS 16.4 (`app/web/vite.config.mjs`); where a guide recommends something newer, the change names the fallback.

### 1.1 accessibility — 4 guides, 37 rows

Rows: Meets 5 · Partly 26 · Does not meet 3 · N/A 3. Per guide: Meets 0 · Partly 3 · Does not meet 1 · N/A 0.

| # | Guide § rule | What the guidance asks (paraphrased) | Verdict | Sev | Our evidence (file:line) | Change if not met |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `accessibility §landmarks` | Put content in header/nav/main/aside/footer used for their real purpose; one main; distinct labels for repeated landmarks; don't label sections into region landmarks. | Partly | Medium | `app/web/src/AppShell.jsx:235` the conversation list (primary content) is an unlabelled `<aside>`; `:237` a second unlabelled `<aside>`; `:236` one `<main>` (good); 16 `<section aria-label>` region landmarks, e.g. `pages/Settings.jsx:405`, `pages/MemoryRoom.jsx:299`, `AppShell.jsx:159` | `AppShell.jsx:235`: make `.list-col` a `<nav aria-labelledby>` (its h1) or a plain `<div>`; give the side column `aria-label="会话资料"`; remove `aria-label` from inner sections that already open with an h3 (`Settings.jsx:405`, `MemoryRoom.jsx:299`, `MemoryHearsay.jsx:41`) |
| 2 | `accessibility §headings` | Sequential real h1–h6, one h1 per page, no fake headings made from div/span. | Partly | Low | Good: list h1 `pages/ChatList.jsx:24`, auth h1 `auth/screens.jsx:34`, chat/page h2, room h3, vault h4 `MemoryRoom.jsx:301,313`; fake headings: group captions are list items `pages/Contacts.jsx:62,81,101`, `pages/ChatList.jsx:150` (`<li className="rows-label">`) | Render each caption as `<h2 className="rows-label">` (h3 inside pages) followed by its own `<ul>` |

| 36 | `accessibility/accessible-generated-content` | No meaning in `content`; decorative glyphs get empty alt (`/ ""`) with a plain fallback first. | Partly | Low | `styles.css:144-145` `.row-more::after { content: " ▾" }` / `" ▸"` read inside the button name `pages/Agents.jsx:154-156`; other `content: ""` decorations fine `styles.css:445,701,709` | `content: " ▾"; content: " ▾" / "";` (the plain line first for older Safari), same for ▸; or replace with an `aria-hidden` ChevronDown icon in JSX |

### 1.2 built-in-ai — 4 guides, 4 rows

Rows: Meets 0 · Partly 0 · Does not meet 0 · N/A 4. Per guide: Meets 0 · Partly 0 · Does not meet 0 · N/A 4.

| # | Guide § rule | What the guidance asks (paraphrased) | Verdict | Sev | Our evidence (file:line) | Change if not met |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `built-in-ai/language-detection` | Detect text language on-device (`LanguageDetector`), create on a user gesture, feature-detect with a remote fallback. | N/A |  | Haiyo's AI runs server-side; no client language detection. The API is Chrome-desktop only, so it is absent from the iOS WKWebView app | (If a per-message `lang` is wanted for screen readers on foreign-language Agent replies, compute it server-side so every device gets it.) |
| 2 | `built-in-ai/language-model` | Prompt API on-device: gesture-gated download with progress, streaming into a polite `aria-busy` region, no innerHTML, sanitise, destroy sessions, cache results. | N/A |  | Agents run server-side on provider models with metering. The guide's output-rendering advice is already met for our server-streamed replies: no innerHTML (`ui/markdownPipeline.js:225`), allow-list tree validated (`ui/markdownClient.js:198-199`), parsed in a worker, answers cached by digest (`markdownClient.js:26-29`, `:72-79`), `aria-busy` while streaming (`pages/AgentChatBits.jsx:262`) and the finished reply announced (`pages/ChatView.jsx:596`) |  |
| 3 | `built-in-ai/summarizer` | On-device `Summarizer` with identical options for availability/create, progress monitor, gesture-gated download, `lang` on output, fallback. | N/A |  | Summaries are made server-side (`app/service/src/agents/summary/`); the on-device API is Chrome/Edge desktop only |  |
| 4 | `built-in-ai/translator` | On-device `Translator` per language pair, gesture-gated download with progress, `lang` on translated container, remote fallback. | N/A |  | No translate feature in the client | (If one is added: translate server-side so the iOS app has it; optionally use `Translator` as a desktop-Chrome enhancement, and always set `lang` on the translated container.) |

Rows: Meets 14 · Partly 13 · Does not meet 9 · N/A 9. Per guide: Meets 5 · Partly 6 · Does not meet 2 · N/A 7.

| 1 | `css/animate-to-intrinsic-sizes` | Animate expanders to and from their natural size with `interpolate-size: allow-keywords` (or `calc-size()`), guard reduced motion, hide collapsed content from assistive tech. | Partly | Low | No size transition anywhere (the only `transition`s are `app/web/src/styles.css:264` and `:344`); expanders snap open: `<details class="thinking">` (`app/web/src/pages/AgentChatBits.jsx:55`, `:181`), whisper fold (`styles.css:478-494`), memory chip (`styles.css:448-453`), 更多 rows (`styles.css:143-145`); no max-height hacks or JS height animation, which is the guide's own fallback. | Optional polish: `:root { interpolate-size: allow-keywords }` and, inside `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)`, `.thinking::details-content { block-size: 0; overflow: clip; transition: block-size .2s, content-visibility .2s allow-discrete }` + `.thinking[open]::details-content { block-size: auto }`; Safari 16.4 / Firefox keep today's instant toggle. |
| 2 | `css/atrule-support-conditionals` | Gate CSS on at-rule support with `@supports at-rule(...)` (or property/selector/`@property` tricks) only when support is narrower than the detector. | N/A |  | No CSS depends on an at-rule's support: `styles.css` has no `@supports`, `@property`, `@function`, `@container` (searched). |  |

| 36 | `css/overflow-clipping-control` | Use `overflow: clip` (no scroll container) instead of `hidden` to clip; `overflow-clip-margin` for bleed; never on scrolling or sticky contexts. | Partly | Low | 24 `overflow: hidden`, no `overflow: clip`; boxes that must never scroll are still scroll containers that `scrollIntoView`/`focus()` (`ui/popover.js:71`, `ui/primitives.jsx:225`) can shift: shell `.app` (`styles.css:80`), `.viewer` (`:1099`), `.viewer-stage` whose zoomed picture overflows it (`:1114`), `.attachment-image` (`:657`), `.avatar-stage` (`:1137`), `.avatar-fallback` (`:91`). | `overflow: clip` on those six (Safari 16, Chrome 90, Firefox 81 — in floor); leave `.md` (`:596`) and real scrollers alone; skip `overflow-clip-margin` (no Safari). |

The keyboard is handled for the auth page by following the visual viewport (`app/web/src/ui/visualViewport.js:54-74`). |  |

| 41 | `css/responsive-design §responsive-typography` | Fluid type with `clamp()` mixing rem and vw, rem bounds, max ≤ 2.5× min; never `vw` alone. | Partly | Low | No `vw` font sizes (good); all sizes fixed px, stepped at 860px (e.g. `.chat-title h2` 23px → 18px, `styles.css:351`, `:1008`; `.list-title h1` 26px, `:126`); `:root { font-size: 14px }` (`:27`) in px, so a larger browser default font size changes nothing. | Headings: `clamp()` values such as `clamp(1.125rem, 1rem + 0.6vw, 1.45rem)` instead of the 860px steps; base size relative to the user's default (e.g. `:root { font-size: 87.5% }`) with px sizes moved to rem — the typography slice should own that decision. |

| 43 | `css/size-aware-styling` | Components respond to their own width via `container-type: inline-size` + `@container`, with a stacked default and media-query fallback where unsupported. | Does not meet | Medium | No `@container`/`container-type` in `styles.css` (searched). Component rules key on the viewport though the main column does not follow it: at 861-1280px windows the details panel overlays (`:981-983`) and main = window − 432px (rail 80 + list 352, `:22-23`), i.e. 429px at 861px — narrower than a 480px phone's column — yet `.file-card` wraps only at ≤480px (`:687`), `.kv-row` stacks only at ≤860px (`:1034-1035`), `.msg-main` stays 72% (`:390` vs `:1012`), `.profile-head > .head-actions` (`:1031`), member-row panels (`:827-830`). | `.main-col { container: main / inline-size }`, `.side-col { container: side / inline-size }`, `.modal-body { container-type: inline-size }`; move those component rules from `@media (max-width: 480px/860px)` to `@container main (max-width: …)` (and `side` for the panel). In floor (Safari 16.0, Chrome 105, Firefox 110), no fallback needed — but see Could not verify (fixed-position Modal inside `.main-col`). |

### 1.4 forms — 27 guides, 60 rows

Rows: Meets 13 · Partly 31 · Does not meet 4 · N/A 12. Per guide: Meets 4 · Partly 12 · Does not meet 0 · N/A 11.

| # | Guide § rule | What the guidance asks (paraphrased) | Verdict | Sev | Our evidence (file:line) | Change if not met |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `forms §form-element` | Wrap controls in `<form>`; name on every control; type=button on helpers; submit is a real submit button; no nested forms. | Partly | Low | Forms everywhere: `auth/screens.jsx:137`, `AgentForm.jsx:575`, `Providers.jsx:853`, `GroupModals.jsx:69`, `Settings.jsx:115,172`, `MemoryRoom.jsx:184,243`, `AddFriendModal.jsx:52`; helpers default `type="button"` `primitives.jsx:103`; `name` only on auth fields `screens.jsx:68-70`, none in AgentForm/Providers/Settings (e.g. `AgentForm.jsx:589,606`); composer is no form, send is `type="button"` `Composer.jsx:377` | Add stable `name`s to AgentForm/Providers/Settings inputs; wrap the composer in `<form onSubmit>` with send `type="submit"` and Enter calling `form.requestSubmit()` |
| 2 | `forms §search-element` | Wrap search and filter controls in `<search>`. | Does not meet | Low | No `<search>`/`role="search"`: `ChatList.jsx:31-37`, `ChatView.jsx:513-518` (input not even `type="search"`), `Providers.jsx:559`, `GroupModals.jsx:16-21` | `<search>` is Safari 17+, above the floor: wrap each in `<div role="search">` now (or `<search>` after raising the floor); `type="search"` at `ChatView.jsx:515` |
| 3 | `forms §fieldset-legend` | Group related controls in fieldset with legend first; no fieldset without legend. | Partly | Low | Legends present `GroupModals.jsx:74-77`, `Providers.jsx:389-390`, `AgentForm.jsx:720-721,728-729`; legend-less fieldsets used only to disable `AgentForm.jsx:762`, `Settings.jsx:411` | Add `<legend className="sr-only">` (e.g. "记忆的细节", "作息") or drop the fieldset and pass `disabled` per input |
| 4 | `forms §submit-labels-no-autosubmit` | Actionable submit text; don't submit on select/radio/checkbox change without an explicit action. | Partly | Low | Action labels good `screens.jsx:172,214,282`, `AgentForm.jsx:780`; memory settings save the moment a Select/switch changes `Settings.jsx:336-348,380-383,412-418`, appellations save on blur `AgentAppellations.jsx:76,84` (deliberate autosave, product rule) and only refusals are said | Keep autosave, but announce each success once ("已保存") through the shared polite region so AT users get confirmation |
| 5 | `forms §selection-control-matrix` | 2–5 exclusive options as visible radios; select only for 6+ or tight space. | Partly | Low | Dropdown for 2–3 options: reply mode `AgentForm.jsx:654`, group mentions `:657`, others budget `:672-681`, proactive level `:699`, new notes `Settings.jsx:441-449`; `.radio-cards` styles exist unused `styles.css:885-891` | Render these as `<fieldset>` radio groups (`.radio-cards`/`.checks`); keep Select for 思考程度 (6) and 记忆用的语言 (15) |
| 6 | `forms §textarea-resize-visual-order` | Don't disable textarea resize without an alternative; don't reorder controls visually. | Meets |  | Composer `resize: none` has its own pointer+keyboard resize handle `styles.css:730`, `Composer.jsx:341-347`; persona `resize: vertical` `styles.css:873`; no CSS `order` (grep) |  |

| 10 | `forms §required-indication` | Mark required fields visually in the label and with the `required` attribute. | Does not meet | Medium | No `required`/`aria-required` and no marker anywhere (grep); AgentForm mixes mandatory (名称, 账号名, 模型) and optional (简介, 擅长领域, 人格文档) fields without saying which `AgentForm.jsx:588-652` | Add `required` to mandatory inputs (forms keep `noValidate`, so no native bubbles, but AT says "required"): `screens.jsx` all fields, AgentForm 名称/账号名, Providers 名称/接口地址/密钥, GroupModals 群名称; `aria-required` on the model picker button; label the few optional fields "（选填）" |

### 1.6 js — 6 guides, 15 rows

Rows: Meets 8 · Partly 4 · Does not meet 0 · N/A 3. Per guide: Meets 2 · Partly 2 · Does not meet 0 · N/A 2.

| # | Guide § rule | What the guidance asks (paraphrased) | Verdict | Sev | Our evidence (file:line) | Change if not met |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `js/js §structuredClone` | Deep-copy with `structuredClone`, not a JSON round-trip, unless JSON normalisation is the point. | Meets |  | grep `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify` in `app/web/src`: none; no clone utilities. |  |
| 2 | `js/js §array-by-copy-and-findLast` | Use `toSorted`/`toReversed`/`toSpliced`/`with` and `findLast`/`findLastIndex` instead of copy-then-mutate or reverse-then-find. | Partly | Low | `findLast` is already used (`data/mentions.js:242`) and is within the floor. Two hot paths still copy and reverse an array just to find its last match: `data/viewmodel.js:580` (all held messages of a conversation, on every view build) and `state/selectors.js:83` (once per list row). Copy-then-sort remains at `data/model.js:187`, `:194`, `:1009`, `data/mentions.js:36`, `:276-277`, `:319`, `ui/reveal.js:96` and `pages/WebSources.jsx:20`. That is correct for our floor: `toSorted`/`toReversed` need Firefox 115 and the target is Firefox 114 (`app/web/vite.config.mjs:17`). Several of these spread a Set or Map iterator and must stay spread anyway. | Replace `[...held].reverse().find(f)` with `held.findLast(f)` (`data/viewmodel.js:580`) and `[...shown].reverse().find(f)` with `shown.findLast(f)` (`state/selectors.js:83`). Move array (not Set or Map) sorts to `toSorted` only once the floor is Firefox 115 or later. |
| 3 | `js/js §groupBy` | Partition iterables with `Object.groupBy`/`Map.groupBy` instead of bucket accumulators; keep `reduce` for aggregations. | Meets |  | There is one bucket loop: pending messages by conversation (`data/viewmodel.js:831-836`). That is exactly `Map.groupBy`, which needs Safari 17.4, Chrome 117 and Firefox 119, all above our floor, so the loop is the right fallback. The other `reduce` calls are scalar aggregations, which the guide says to keep (`state/selectors.js:107`, `pages/ChatView.jsx:54`). Revisit once the floor reaches Safari 17.4. |  |
| 4 | `js/js §Promise.withResolvers` | Use `Promise.withResolvers()` when resolve or reject must be stored outside the executor. | Meets |  | Resolvers are stored from inside executors (`data/actions.js:269-273`, `ui/markdownClient.js:276-285`), which is the floor-safe form; `Promise.withResolvers` needs Safari 17.4, Chrome 119 and Firefox 121. Revisit with the floor. |  |
| 5 | `js/js §AbortSignal.timeout-any` | Use `AbortSignal.timeout` for deadlines and `AbortSignal.any` to combine one with user cancel, not a controller plus `setTimeout`. | Partly | Low | There are no hand-rolled abort timers to replace, and uploads honour a caller's signal (`api/http.js:136-156`). But ordinary JSON calls have no deadline at all (`api/http.js:55-73`, `:84-93`), so on a stalled mobile connection a request can stay pending forever. | In `request` and `requestBytes` (`app/web/src/api/http.js:65`, `:88`), pass `signal: AbortSignal.timeout(60_000)` (floor-safe: Safari 16, Chrome 103, Firefox 100) and map `TimeoutError` to `ApiError(0, "timeout")`. Combining it with a caller's signal through `AbortSignal.any` needs Safari 17.4, so feature-detect that. |
| 6 | `js/js §error-cause` | Wrap rethrown errors with `{ cause: err }`; custom Error subclasses forward their options to `super`. | Partly | Low | `request` and `requestBytes` catch the fetch failure and throw a new `ApiError(0, "network_error")`, discarding the original error (`api/http.js:66-67`, `:89-90`). `ApiError`'s constructor neither takes nor forwards options (`:11-12`). Error cause is within the floor. | Give `ApiError` a trailing `options` parameter, passed on to `super(message, options)` (`api/http.js:11-12`). At `:66` and `:89`, use `catch (err) { throw new ApiError(0, "network_error", null, {}, { cause: err }); }`. Keep the cause out of anything users see. |
| 7 | `js/js §URLSearchParams` | Parse and build query strings with `URLSearchParams` or `URL.searchParams`, not manual split and decode. | Meets |  | Built with `URLSearchParams` (`api/routes.js:7-14`) and read with it (`auth/router.js:20`, `:104`); grep `split("&")\|decodeURIComponent`: none. Two single-parameter avatar URLs use `encodeURIComponent` (`api/routes.js:68`, `:221`), which is equivalent. |  |
| 8 | `js/js §TextEncoder-TextDecoder` | Convert UTF-8 bytes with `TextEncoder`/`TextDecoder`, not `fromCharCode` or `escape` tricks. | Meets |  | `TextEncoder` measures byte weights (`data/memoryWeight.js:5`). `String.fromCharCode` only builds single sentinel code points (`ui/mentionMarks.js:14-21`), not byte decoding. |  |
| 9 | `js/js §replaceChildren` | Clear or replace children with `replaceChildren()`, not `innerHTML = ''` or removal loops. | N/A |  | React owns the DOM. grep `innerHTML\|removeChild\|replaceChildren` in `app/web/src`: none, and the Markdown pipeline never hands HTML to `innerHTML` (`ui/markdownPipeline.js:225`). |  |
| 10 | `js/js §event-constructors` | Use `new Event` or `new CustomEvent` with explicit bubbles/cancelable; never `createEvent`/`initEvent`. | Meets |  | `window.dispatchEvent(new Event(CHANGED))` (`state/appHistory.js:13`); grep `createEvent\|initEvent\|initCustomEvent`: none. |  |
| 11 | `js/calculate-event-differentials` | Compute elapsed or remaining time with Temporal (`since`/`until`, `largestUnit`) and format it with `Intl.DurationFormat`, not Date math or hand-made unit strings. | Meets |  | There is one countdown: a friend request's validity, "N 分钟/小时内有效", computed from the milliseconds between two instants (`data/viewmodel.js:1045-1052`). That is exact for minutes and hours, with no calendar units and no DST exposure. Temporal is missing across our floor, and `Intl.DurationFormat` needs Chrome 129 and Firefox 136 (our floor is 111 and 114), so hand-written Chinese units are the floor-safe form. |  |
| 12 | `js/capture-location-agnostic-data` | Keep dates and times that must not move with the time zone (birthdays, alarms, expiries) as Temporal Plain types; never hand-build ISO strings. | N/A |  | The client only handles server instants, shown in local time (`data/viewmodel.js:12-48`). The one "day" it shows is the local day an instant falls on, which is the intended meaning (`data/errorTexts.js:139-145`). grep for birthdays or plain dates: none. |  |
| 13 | `js/coordinate-global-events` | Tie zone-specific times to IANA zones with DST disambiguation; use `toLocaleString` for times users see, not manual padding. | Partly | Low | The server side is sound: the free-allowance day uses a validated IANA zone (`app/service/src/config.ts:693-702`) and computes the zone's midnights with an offset loop (`app/service/src/accounts/store.ts:41-79`), tested across a New York DST change (`app/service/tests/accounts.test.ts:53-62`). Temporal is not available unflagged in Node 24 (`Dockerfile:1`). Client times are hand-padded, always 24-hour, in three separate copies (`data/viewmodel.js:9-48`, `pages/Usage.jsx:10-17`, `data/errorTexts.js:172-186`). | Replace the three `pad()`/`getHours()` formatters with one module of cached `Intl.DateTimeFormat("zh-CN", …)` formatters (floor-safe), e.g. `{hour: "2-digit", minute: "2-digit", hourCycle: "h23"}`, `{month: "numeric", day: "numeric"}`, and year/month/day/weekday for the bubble's title. Keep the 今天/昨天/今年 branching. Move the server math to `Temporal.ZonedDateTime` only once the Node runtime ships it. |
| 14 | `js/sort-high-precision-timestamps` | Sort and compare recorded high-precision timestamps with `Temporal.Instant`, never with millisecond-truncated `Date` values. | Meets |  | Messages are ordered by the server's integer `seq`, not by timestamps (`data/model.js:187`, `:194`). Conversation order uses millisecond activity with deterministic tie-breakers (`data/viewmodel.js:935`). No sub-millisecond data is parsed. |  |
| 15 | `js/support-global-calendar-systems` | For non-Gregorian calendars, use `withCalendar`, `monthCode`, `monthsInYear`, `PlainDate.compare` and `toLocaleString`. | N/A |  | The Chinese UI shows Gregorian dates only (`<html lang="zh-CN">`, `app/web/index.html:2`). There is no calendar arithmetic beyond day boundaries and no lunar dates. |  |

### 1.7 performance — 29 guides, 53 rows

Rows: Meets 12 · Partly 16 · Does not meet 5 · N/A 20. Per guide: Meets 1 · Partly 8 · Does not meet 4 · N/A 16.

| # | Guide § rule | What the guidance asks (paraphrased) | Verdict | Sev | Our evidence (file:line) | Change if not met |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `performance/performance §critical-css` | Inline above-the-fold CSS in the head, defer the rest; split stylesheets by `media` so unused ones do not block render. | Partly | Low | One render-blocking stylesheet for every screen, sign-in included (`app/web/src/main.jsx:4` → built `dist/assets/index-mhW_p4TR.css`, 61 KB raw / ~13 KB gzip, in the head); nothing inline; `#root` is empty until JS runs (`app/web/index.html:11`); desktop and phone rules share the file (`app/web/src/styles.css:981`, `:986`) | Low value for a client-rendered SPA. Cheapest win: put a tiny inline `<style>` and a static "正在加载…" placeholder inside `#root` in `app/web/index.html`, so the first paint is not blank while JS loads. Optional: import shell-only CSS from `AppShell.jsx`, so Vite moves it out of the sign-in path. No media split needed at this size |
| 2 | `performance/performance §async-defer-module-scripts` | Non-critical scripts use `async`/`defer`; `type="module"` is deferred by default. | Meets |  | The only script is `<script type="module">` (`app/web/index.html:12`; the built head has one module script, so it is deferred) |  |
| 3 | `performance/performance §resource-hints` | Preconnect to key third-party origins; preload what the current view needs; prefetch the next view's bundle. | Partly | Medium | No third-party origins exist, so preconnect is moot (CSP `default-src 'self'`, `app/service/src/web.ts:15-25`). But the signed-in shell chunk (`AppShell-*.js`, ~101 KB gzip) is requested only after `GET /api/me` resolves (`app/web/src/auth/AuthRoot.jsx:53-61`, `:108-110`; `app/web/src/App.jsx:9-10`): HTML → entry JS → /api/me → shell chunk. The sync does start in parallel with the shell (`AuthRoot.jsx:73-82`) | In `App.jsx` call `loadShell()` once at startup when the path is not an auth route (`auth/router.js` `parseRoute`), so the chunk downloads alongside `/api/me`. Sign-in/register/reset pages still never fetch it; a signed-out visitor landing on `/` pays ~101 KB. Optional: warm the Markdown worker at idle after the shell mounts |
| 4 | `performance/performance §no-css-import` | Never use `@import` in CSS: it creates sequential request chains. | Meets |  | No `@import` in `app/web/src/styles.css` (grep); 0 in the built `index-*.css` and `Markdown-*.css` |  |
| 5 | `performance/performance §no-heavy-head-js / tree-shake-minify` | Keep large non-critical JS out of a blocking head; tree-shake and minify before deploy. | Meets |  | Vite production build (minified, tree-shaken); the head script is a deferred module; icons come in as named re-exports, so they tree-shake (`app/web/src/icons.js:1-64`) |  |
| 6 | `performance/performance §lcp-in-html / no-js-hero-loader` | The LCP element should be in the raw HTML; avoid client-side rendering chains (HTML → JS → execute → content) for the hero. | Partly | Medium | Everything is client-rendered (empty `#root`, `index.html:11`). The LCP (auth card heading, or the chat list text) waits for the entry chunk (378 KB / ~121 KB gzip), then `/api/me`, then for signed-in users the shell chunk and `/api/sync` | An SPA trade-off; shorten the chain instead: the parallel shell fetch from row 3, plus the inline placeholder from row 1. Adding SSR is out of scope |
| 7 | `performance/performance §fetchpriority-lcp-image` | Put `fetchpriority="high"` on the LCP image (and its preload); never on more than one or two. | N/A |  | No image is an LCP candidate (LCP is text); `fetchpriority` is used nowhere (grep) |  |
| 8 | `performance/performance §lazy-loading` | Lazy-load offscreen images and iframes; never lazy-load the LCP or above-the-fold image. | Meets |  | Avatars and chat images use `loading="lazy"` (`app/web/src/ui/primitives.jsx:16`, `:61`; `app/web/src/pages/Messages.jsx:96`); LCP is text. Visible rail and header avatars are lazy too, which costs nothing here because every image is JS-rendered anyway |  |
| 9 | `performance/performance §inp-main-thread (break up tasks, separate UI from heavy work)` | Split work over 50 ms, yield with `scheduler.yield()` plus fallback, give immediate UI feedback and push heavy computation elsewhere. | Partly | Medium | Every push, stream deltas included (the service sends at most one per 50 ms, `app/service/src/agents/runtime/stream.ts:7`), does this: `setModel` → emit (`app/web/src/data/store.jsx:89-98`; delta `app/web/src/data/sync.js:631-633`) → synchronous `useSyncExternalStore` render (`store.jsx:171`) → a full `toView` over every conversation × held message (`app/web/src/data/viewmodel.js:779-934`; 30+ held per conversation, `data/model.js:60`) → every `useStore()` consumer re-renders (77 call sites in 31 files). `MessageItem` (`pages/Messages.jsx:180-181`) and `Avatar` (`ui/primitives.jsx:19-20`) are not memoised. Fresh `mentions` arrays (`viewmodel.js:488`) defeat `AgentText`'s memo (`ui/AgentText.jsx:161`). No yielding anywhere (grep for scheduler, postTask, startTransition: none) | (a) In `createDataLayer`, apply pushes to `model` at once but emit at most once per animation frame (rAF, with a setTimeout fallback). (b) Make `toView` incremental: cache each conversation's view and message views by their model objects (WeakMap), so unchanged ones keep identity. (c) `React.memo` `MessageItem`, the ChatList row and `Avatar`; give the streaming bubble its own subscription to `model.streams`. (d) Only if a measured task still exceeds 50 ms, yield in the data layer (`scheduler.yield()`, with a setTimeout fallback, since Safari lacks the Scheduler API) |

Then portal `Modal` (row 15) and add it to `.messages` (`styles.css:387`); this must come after the portal because `ProposalEditor`'s modal renders inside a message (`AgentChatBits.jsx:685`). Works on Safari 15.4+ |

The late shell chunk is row 3 |  |

The guide's a11y advice is already met: `aria-busy` on the streaming bubble (`pages/AgentChatBits.jsx:262`, `:318`), finished reply announced politely (`pages/ChatView.jsx:596`) |  |

Apply non-urgent post-connect results (runs, models, Agent cards) via `scheduler.postTask(…, {priority:'background'})`, feature-detected, falling back to `setTimeout(…, 0)` (Safari has no Scheduler API) |

### 1.8 privacy — 1 guide, 24 rows

Rows: Meets 13 · Partly 3 · Does not meet 3 · N/A 5. Per guide: Meets 0 · Partly 1 · Does not meet 0 · N/A 0.

| # | Guide § rule | What the guidance asks (paraphrased) | Verdict | Sev | Our evidence (file:line) | Change if not met |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `privacy/privacy §principles: protective by default` | Privacy-protective defaults; no privacy labour or consent fatigue; no dark patterns; act as the user's agent. | Meets |  | No analytics, trackers, cookie banners or consent dialogs (CSP allows only own origin, `app/service/src/web.ts:15-25`); Better Auth telemetry off and no IP tracking (`app/service/src/auth/auth.ts:240,262`); reply cost display is opt-in and local only (`app/web/src/data/prefs.js:1-5`) |  |
| 2 | `privacy/privacy §1 minimise collection` | Collect only what the task needs, at the lowest granularity; nothing "just in case". | Meets |  | Registration asks invite code, email, password, nickname, handle only (`app/web/src/auth/screens.jsx:215-229`); no birthday/phone/address; client address not stored on sessions (`app/service/src/auth/auth.ts:260-262`) |  |
| 3 | `privacy/privacy §1 delete when the purpose ends` | Delete data as soon as its purpose is fulfilled. | Meets |  | Whispers expire after 7 days and are deleted 30 days later, memory change log 30 days, web-search day counts after a week (`app/service/src/app.ts:205-231`); WS tickets 30 s (`app/service/src/sync/tickets.ts:4`); verify link 24 h, reset 1 h (`app/service/src/auth/mail-copy.ts:5-6`); form drafts in memory only (`app/web/src/data/drafts.js:1-4`). Account-level deletion: row 60 |  |
| 4 | `privacy/privacy §1 guest checkout` | Offer guest checkout instead of forced accounts. | N/A |  | Invite-only social app; no commerce flow |  |
| 5 | `privacy/privacy §1 noise for aggregates` | Add noise (differential privacy) when gathering aggregate statistics. | N/A |  | No aggregate user statistics are gathered or shared; logs keep operational counts only (e.g. `app/service/src/app.ts:228`) |  |
| 6 | `privacy/privacy §1 purpose limitation` | Never reuse data collected for one purpose for another without fresh consent. | Meets |  | Chat content goes only to the model the owner picked, stated beside the choice (`app/web/src/pages/ModelPicker.jsx:22`, `app/web/src/pages/Providers.jsx:76`); Agent memory about the owner is visible, editable and vault-able (记忆宫殿 `app/web/src/pages/Settings.jsx:70`; `newNotes` auto/vault/open `app/service/src/session.ts:74`). Product judgement; consent withdrawal for the store build is planned (`docs/design/2026-11-24-store-app-draft.md:189`) |  |
| 7 | `privacy/privacy §2 inline reasons, context before permissions` | Explain next to the field why data is asked; give reason and context before any powerful permission prompt. | Meets |  | Hints say who sees nickname and handle (`app/web/src/auth/screens.jsx:224-228`); data-flow notice beside model choice (`app/web/src/pages/ModelPicker.jsx:22`); the web app requests no browser permission (grep `requestPermission\|getUserMedia\|geolocation`: none) |  |
| 8 | `privacy/privacy §2 PEPC <permission>` | Consider the declarative `<permission>` element for user-initiated permission requests. | N/A |  | No permission prompts at all (row 44) |  |
| 9 | `privacy/privacy §2 transparency of practices` | Say clearly why data is collected, where it goes and how long it is kept, without burying it in a long policy. | Partly | Medium | Where chat content goes is stated inline (`app/web/src/pages/ModelPicker.jsx:22`); nothing tells users what is stored, for how long, or about the mail sender and web-search engine; no privacy page (planned S2, `docs/design/2026-11-24-store-app-draft.md:188,493`); `SECURITY.md` is stale (mentions LINE/Element, says no e-mail verification, `SECURITY.md:21,35`) | Add a short plain-language 「数据与隐私」 page, linked from 设置 and the register screen: what is stored (account, messages, files, Agent memory), who receives it (chosen model provider, web-search engine when used, mail sender), retention (row 40), how to export/delete; update `SECURITY.md` |
| 10 | `privacy/privacy §2 Clear-Site-Data on logout` | Send `Clear-Site-Data` when a user logs out, from a subresource rather than the main navigation. | Does not meet | Low | Same as row 37 | See row 37 |
| 11 | `privacy/privacy §3 scrub PII from logs` | Mask emails, tokens and IDs in application logs automatically. | Partly | Low | Same as row 25: tokens/queries/file names masked, client IP logged per request (`app/service/src/app.ts:295`); no e-mail in log calls (grep `log.*email`: none) | See row 25 |
| 12 | `privacy/privacy §3 HttpOnly session cookies` | Session identifiers in `HttpOnly` cookies so scripts cannot read them. | Meets |  | `httpOnly: true` (`app/service/src/auth/auth.ts:267`; `app/service/tests/origin.test.ts:265`); session token removed from sign-in JSON (`app/service/src/auth/auth.ts:347-360`) |  |
| 13 | `privacy/privacy §3 rate-limit scraping-prone endpoints` | Rate-limit user search and profile views to prevent bulk scraping. | Meets |  | People lookup is exact-handle only and per-principal limited (`app/service/src/principals/routes.ts:104-125`); sign-in/reset limited per address+email and per address (`app/service/src/auth/auth.ts:333-341`) |  |
| 14 | `privacy/privacy §3 CHIPS, no unpartitioned SameSite=None` | Use `Partitioned` cookies for 1:1 embeds; never rely on unpartitioned `SameSite=None`. | Meets |  | Only first-party SameSite=Lax cookies (`app/service/src/auth/auth.ts:267`); the app is never embedded (`app/service/src/web.ts:24`), so no partitioned cookie is needed |  |
| 15 | `privacy/privacy §3 no tokens/PII in localStorage` | Never keep sensitive tokens or PII in `localStorage`. | Meets |  | localStorage holds one display flag (`app/web/src/data/prefs.js:1-5`); sessionStorage holds whisper ids and text digests, never text (`app/web/src/ui/slowTexts.jsx:1-4`); drafts with API keys stay in memory (`app/web/src/data/drafts.js:1-4`) |  |
| 16 | `privacy/privacy §4 third-party audit` | Audit third-party requests; no third-party scripts where sensitive data is handled; static share links instead of social SDKs. | Meets |  | No third-party scripts, SDKs, share widgets or embeds; CSP forbids other origins (`app/service/src/web.ts:15-25`); grep of `app/web/src` for external hosts finds only provider URL examples (`app/web/src/pages/Providers.jsx:22-25`) |  |
| 17 | `privacy/privacy §4 facades and no-cookie embeds` | Load heavy embeds only on click behind a static facade; prefer privacy-preserving embed hosts. | N/A |  | No video or social embeds (grep `<iframe`: none) |  |
| 18 | `privacy/privacy §4 FedCM` | Use FedCM to mediate federated sign-in. | N/A |  | No federated sign-in: email + password only (`app/service/src/auth/auth.ts:404-410`) |  |
| 19 | `privacy/privacy §5 Permissions-Policy` | Disable powerful features by default with `Permissions-Policy`. | Meets |  | `camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=()` (`app/service/src/web.ts:47`); optional additions: `accelerometer=(), payment=(), usb=()` |  |
| 20 | `privacy/privacy §5 strict Referrer-Policy` | A strict `Referrer-Policy` so URL parameters do not leak to third parties. | Meets |  | `same-origin` header and meta (`app/service/src/web.ts:45`, `app/web/index.html:5`); the reset token leaves the address bar before any request (`app/web/src/auth/AuthRoot.jsx:21`) |  |
| 21 | `privacy/privacy §6 no fingerprinting, feature detection, UA-CH` | No canvas/font/device fingerprinting; feature detection instead of UA sniffing; UA Client Hints only when device targeting is needed. | Meets |  | grep `navigator.userAgent\|userAgentData\|enumerateDevices\|toDataURL\|getImageData` in `app/web/src`: none; capability checks are feature-based (`app/web/src/ui/tapOpen.js:1-9`); the iOS shell is to be detected by its message handler, not the UA (`docs/design/2026-11-24-store-app-draft.md:167-168`) |  |
| 22 | `privacy/privacy §7 access / export` | Give users a clear way to access all data collected about them. | Does not meet | Medium | No export route in the API (all `/api/*` paths listed via grep of `app/service/src`: none for export); `SECURITY.md:39` lists export as pre-launch work | Add `GET /api/me/export` (rate-limited, streamed JSON/zip: profile, own messages, own uploads, Agents and their memory notes) and a 「导出我的数据」 row under 我的资料 (`app/web/src/pages/Settings.jsx:213-218`) |
| 23 | `privacy/privacy §7 deletion as easy as sign-up` | Easy self-service account and data deletion, without contacting support; as easy as signing up. | Does not meet | High | No `DELETE /api/me` or deletion UI (route grep; 我的资料 has only 退出登录, `app/web/src/pages/Settings.jsx:213-218`); `SECURITY.md:35` says no self-service deletion; App Store 5.1.1(v) requires in-app deletion for the iOS app (`docs/design/2026-11-24-store-app-draft.md:190`), planned last as S7 (`:322,427-448`) | Build S7 before the store submission: 设置 › 我的资料 › 删除账号 (password re-entry, explicit confirm); server deletes account, sessions, uploads/avatars, Agents and memory, applies the chosen group-message rule, closes sockets; link it from the privacy page (row 46) |

### 1.9 pwa — 10 guides, 10 rows

Rows: Meets 0 · Partly 2 · Does not meet 4 · N/A 4. Per guide: Meets 0 · Partly 2 · Does not meet 4 · N/A 4.

| # | Guide § rule | What the guidance asks (paraphrased) | Verdict | Sev | Our evidence (file:line) | Change if not met |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `pwa/pwa` | (Hub, frontmatter only: lists the nine PWA guides below.) Build installable, offline-capable apps that integrate with the OS. | Does not meet | Low | No manifest, service worker, `theme-color` or `apple-touch-icon` (`app/web/index.html:1-14`; grep `serviceWorker\|manifest` in `app/web/src`: none). The iOS route is a native WKWebView shell instead (`docs/reviews/2026-11-24-ios-shell-review.md:1-8`). | Start with metadata only (row 43). Add a service worker only if offline loading or web push becomes a product goal. |
| 2 | `pwa/app-badging-and-notifications` | (Frontmatter only, no body.) Show unread counts on the app icon; deliver background notifications that open the right view. | Does not meet | Medium | The unread total exists (`state/selectors.js:106-108`) but appears only in the in-page tab badge (`AppShell.jsx:46`). The tab title is always "Haiyo" (`app/web/index.html:8`; no `document.title` writes in `app/web/src`). No Badging, Notification or Push API is used. The iOS shell plans native `badge.set`/`push.register` bridge calls from the web side (S9, `docs/design/2026-11-24-store-app-draft.md:324`, `:425`), not yet wired in `app/web/src`. | (1) Mirror `unreadTotal` into `document.title` (e.g. `(3) Haiyo`) from one effect in `AppShell.jsx`; this works in every browser and in background tabs. (2) Next to it, call `navigator.setAppBadge?.(n)` / `clearAppBadge?.()`, feature-detected; it takes effect once the app is installed. (3) Wire the shell's `badge.set` as S9 plans. (4) Web push needs a service worker and a permission prompt: a product decision, so ask first. |
| 3 | `pwa/background-sync-and-transfers` | (Frontmatter only.) Queue actions done offline and finish them later, even after the app closes; refresh content in the background. | Partly | Low | Sends are optimistic, with a pending queue, but a failed send waits for a manual retry (`data/actions.js:309-316`). Uploads queue for a limited number of slots (`:266-274`). Reconnects use a 1–30 s backoff (`data/sync.js:84-88`) and nothing listens for `online` (grep `"online"`: none), so after the network returns the app can wait up to 30 s. Nothing continues after the tab closes (no service worker). | In `retry()` in `data/sync.js`, listen once for `window` `online`, and for `visibilitychange` to visible, then cancel the backoff timer and connect at once. On reconnect, automatically retry pending sends that failed with `network_error` (status 0), and keep the manual 重试 for refusals. Skip Background Sync/Fetch: they are Chromium-only and need a service worker. |
| 4 | `pwa/custom-window-title-bar` | (Frontmatter only.) Title-bar content and drag regions for installed desktop apps (Window Controls Overlay). | N/A |  | Not installable (no manifest), so there is no standalone desktop window. |  |
| 5 | `pwa/install-web-app` | Offer install in the page via `<install>`, `navigator.install()` or `beforeinstallprompt`, behind a manifest with a stable `id`; hide the button where unsupported. | N/A |  | There is no in-page install offer; the native iOS app is the install path (`docs/design/2026-11-24-store-app-draft.md`). The manifest this needs is covered in row 43. If an install button is ever added, hide it inside the iOS shell. |  |
| 6 | `pwa/installable-web-app-metadata` | (Frontmatter only.) Manifest identity, adaptive icons, display mode, shortcuts and localised presentation for a standalone install. | Does not meet | Low | `index.html` links only a PNG favicon (`app/web/index.html:7`). There is no `<link rel="manifest">`, no `apple-touch-icon` (so iOS Add to Home Screen falls back to a page screenshot) and no `theme-color`. Only one icon file exists (`app/web/public/assets/agent-family-mark-v2.png`). The CSP's `default-src 'self'` already allows a same-origin manifest (`app/service/src/web.ts:15-16`), and unhashed files are served `no-cache` (`:90-92`). | Add `app/web/public/manifest.webmanifest` with `"id": "/"`, `"name"`/`"short_name": "Haiyo"`, `"lang": "zh-CN"`, `"start_url": "/"`, `"display": "standalone"`, `background_color`/`theme_color` `#f1effd` (`styles.css:45`), and icons at 192 and 512 plus a maskable 512. In `app/web/index.html`, link it and add `<link rel="apple-touch-icon">` and `<meta name="theme-color" content="#f1effd">`. The logo is still undecided, so use the current mark and swap it later. |
| 7 | `pwa/migrate-web-app-origin` | Move an installed app to a same-site origin with `migrate_from` and `.well-known/web-app-origin-association`; data and permissions don't move. | N/A |  | Nothing is installed to migrate. Worth knowing: the possible move from haiyo.org to haiyo.ai is cross-site, which this API cannot handle anyway, and the server refuses dot segments such as `.well-known` in static paths (`app/service/src/web.ts:56-64`). |  |
| 8 | `pwa/offline-loading-and-updates` | (Frontmatter only.) Load reliably offline or on slow networks; roll out new versions without disrupting sessions. | Partly | Low | Updates: after a deploy, a missing chunk triggers one guarded reload (`app/web/src/staleChunk.js:5-27`, `main.jsx:7`). That reload loses every in-memory draft: composer text (`pages/Composer.jsx:62`, from store state) and form drafts (`data/drafts.js:1-4`). Hashed assets are cached as immutable and `index.html` revalidates (`app/service/src/web.ts:90-92`). Offline: there is no service worker, so reloading offline shows the browser's error page; a live session shows a connection-failure line (`data/store.jsx:80-102`). The iOS shell has its own offline screen (`docs/reviews/2026-11-24-ios-shell-review.md:26`). | Before the stale-chunk reload, save composer drafts to `sessionStorage` and restore them after it. Leave provider form drafts out: `drafts.js` deliberately keeps API keys out of storage. A service worker for an offline shell is optional and a separate decision. |
| 9 | `pwa/os-content-sharing` | (Frontmatter only.) Send content through the OS share sheet (Web Share) and receive shares (`share_target`). | Does not meet | Low | No `navigator.share` (grep: none). The image viewer offers only download and open-in-new-tab (`ui/ImageViewer.jsx:252-255`), so in iPhone Safari a picture goes to Files, not Photos. `Share2` is imported but never used (`icons.js:53`). Receiving shares needs an installed manifest with `share_target` (Chromium only). | Add a 分享 button to the viewer bar, shown only when `navigator.canShare?.({ files: [file] })` is true. Prefetch the picture as a `File` when the viewer shows it, then call `navigator.share({ files: [file] })` directly in the click handler so Safari keeps the user activation. Check first whether the iOS shell's native download sheet already covers this. |
| 10 | `pwa/os-launch-and-file-handling` | (Frontmatter only.) Open the installed app for associated files or protocol links (`file_handlers`, `launch_handler`, `protocol_handlers`). | N/A |  | Not installable. Links into conversations are plain URLs (`state/location.js`), and the iOS shell handles universal links natively (`docs/reviews/2026-11-24-ios-shell-review.md:23`). |  |

Rows: Meets 9 · Partly 8 · Does not meet 12 · N/A 8. Per guide: Meets 2 · Partly 3 · Does not meet 7 · N/A 5.

| # | Guide § rule | What the guidance asks (paraphrased) | Verdict | Sev | Our evidence (file:line) | Change if not met |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `security/client-side-encryption` | Establish shared keys with WebCrypto KEMs (hybrid ML-KEM) or X25519, encrypt payloads in the browser with AEAD and fresh IVs. | N/A |  | No end-to-end encryption by design (Agents must read messages on the server; `SECURITY.md:21` says chats are plaintext in the DB); grep `subtle` in `app/web/src`: none, only `crypto.randomUUID` (`app/web/src/data/store.jsx:75`) |  |
| 2 | `security/digital-signatures` | Sign and verify messages/artifacts in the browser with ML-DSA or Ed25519, with context-based domain separation and fallbacks. | N/A |  | Nothing is signed or verified client-side (same grep as row 1); integrity of uploads/links is handled server-side |  |
| 3 | `security/local-network-access` | Public pages reaching LAN/loopback devices must gate on the LNA permission, declare `targetAddressSpace`, and use HTTPS. | N/A |  | The browser only talks to its own origin: `connect-src 'self' wss://<host>` (`app/service/src/web.ts:19,43`); self-hosted model providers are fetched by the server through `guardedFetch`, loopback only in tests (`app/service/src/app.ts:150-155`) |  |
| 4 | `security/passkey-authentication` | Offer discoverable passkey sign-in: Conditional UI autofill (`username webauthn`, conditional get on load) plus a button; 404 → `signalUnknownCredential`. | Does not meet | Medium | Sign-in is email + password only: email `autocomplete="username"`, password `current-password`, no `autoFocus` (`app/web/src/auth/screens.jsx:173-178`); exposed auth routes are an allow-list with no passkey routes (`app/service/src/auth/auth.ts:404-410`); grep `PublicKeyCredential\|navigator.credentials` in `app/web/src`: none | After passkey-registration (row 8): add the passkey routes to `AUTH_ENDPOINTS`; in `SignIn` give the email input `autoComplete="username webauthn"` and `autoFocus` (dedicated page) with `aria-describedby` on the title; start `navigator.credentials.get({publicKey, mediation:'conditional', signal})` when `getClientCapabilities().conditionalGet`; add a 「用通行密钥登录」 button that aborts the conditional call first; server returns 404 for unknown credential IDs; client calls `signalUnknownCredential` (feature-detected) with the server `rpId`; load `webauthn-polyfills` for the Safari 16.4 floor |
| 5 | `security/passkey-conditional-create` | Right after a full password sign-in, abort autofill and silently create a passkey with `mediation:'conditional'` if `conditionalCreate`; swallow errors. | Does not meet | Low | `SignIn.submit` goes from `api.signIn` straight to `getMe` and navigate (`app/web/src/auth/screens.jsx:153-158`); no passkeys exist | Once row 8 exists: after `onSignedIn(me)` in `SignIn.submit`, if capabilities.conditionalCreate, fetch creation options (server `rp.id`, `excludeCredentials`) and call `navigator.credentials.create({publicKey, mediation:'conditional'})`, silently ignoring InvalidState/NotAllowed/Abort; server relaxes user-presence only for this path; `signalUnknownCredential` if verification fails |
| 6 | `security/passkey-management` | Settings list of passkeys (AAGUID name/icon, created, last used, rename, delete); `signalAllAcceptedCredentials` on load/delete, `signalCurrentUserDetails` on rename. | Does not meet | Low | 我的资料 has avatar, name, handle, email and sign-out only (`app/web/src/pages/Settings.jsx:209-218`); `PATCH /api/me` renames displayName/handle (`app/service/src/principals/routes.ts:30-44`) with nothing to sync | With row 8: add a 「通行密钥」 list under 我的资料 (server returns `rpId`, base64url `userId`, credentials); on delete call `signalAllAcceptedCredentials`, on name/handle change call `signalCurrentUserDetails` (both feature-detected, `console.warn` on failure, never `location.hostname` for rpId) |
| 7 | `security/passkey-reauthentication` | Step-up before sensitive actions with `allowCredentials` limited to the signed-in user's passkeys; server checks the credential belongs to that user. | Does not meet | Low | No step-up anywhere today; sensitive steps are provider API-key entry (`app/web/src/pages/Providers.jsx:884`) and the planned account deletion "输密码、明确确认" (`docs/design/2026-11-24-store-app-draft.md:448`) | When account deletion (S7) lands, offer passkey step-up (`allowCredentials` = this user's IDs; server verifies `credential.userId === session user`) with password as fallback; never `uiMode:'immediate'` for this |
