### 1.11 ui-atoms — 14 guides, 14 rows

Rows: Meets 2 · Partly 7 · Does not meet 0 · N/A 5. Per guide: Meets 2 · Partly 7 · Does not meet 0 · N/A 5.

| # | Guide § rule | What the guidance asks (paraphrased) | Verdict | Sev | Our evidence (file:line) | Change if not met |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `ui-atoms/carousel-slide-effects` | Scroll-snapped carousel whose slides animate on a `view()` timeline; feature-detect, respect reduced motion, make the scroller focusable and named. | N/A |  | No carousel or scroller of slides: the image viewer swaps one picture at a time by keys, buttons or swipe (`app/web/src/ui/ImageViewer.jsx:3`, `:184-186`); grep `scroll-snap\|animation-timeline` in `app/web/src/styles.css`: none. |  |
| 2 | `ui-atoms/component-specific-light-dark-theme` | Put `color-scheme` on self-contained surfaces that must stay dark or light (media viewers, code blocks) so built-in UI matches; only on elements with a background. | Partly | Low | The page has no dark mode (no `color-scheme`/`light-dark` anywhere in `app/web/src/styles.css`). The image viewer is a dark surface with its own tokens and background (`styles.css:1093-1099`) but no `color-scheme`, so any native UI inside it (scrollbar, default focus ring, a future control) draws in light style. Almost no native UI there today. | Add `color-scheme: dark;` to `.viewer` (`app/web/src/styles.css:1093`). It has its own background, which the guide requires. Keep the explicit `color` on `.viewer-state .btn` (`:1119`). |
| 3 | `ui-atoms/custom-list-markers` | (Guide is draft: stub: author notes only, covering `list-style` strings, `::marker` caveats, icon markers, subgrid and step lists.) | N/A |  | draft: stub in the guidance. For reference: custom dots via `::before` on `.share-items li` (`styles.css:701`); Markdown lists use `disc`/`decimal` (`styles.css:604-605`). |  |
| 4 | `ui-atoms/icons` | Choose a CSS mask, `<use>` or inline SVG; icons follow currentColor with ≥3:1 contrast; hide decorative ones; name icon-only controls; masked icons need `forced-color-adjust: none`. | Partly | Low | lucide-react inline SVG, imported through one file (`app/web/src/icons.js:1-64`), which adds `aria-hidden="true"` when no a11y prop is given (`app/web/node_modules/lucide-react/dist/esm/shared/src/build/buildLucideIconNode.mjs:47-49`). Icon-only buttons are named by `IconBtn` (`ui/primitives.jsx:120`). Full-colour brand marks are `<img alt="">` (`ui/ModelBrand.jsx:56`). Single-colour marks are a CSS mask on `currentColor` (`ModelBrand.jsx:58`, `styles.css:252-255`) with no `forced-color-adjust: none`, so in forced-colours mode the OpenAI/Moonshot/Grok marks take the forced background colour and vanish. | Add `forced-color-adjust: none;` to `span.model-brand` (`app/web/src/styles.css:252`). Nothing else is needed: the marks are decorative and the model name sits next to them. |
| 5 | `ui-atoms/position-aware-tooltips` | When an anchored tooltip flips, restyle its arrow or edge for the fallback in use; don't let a tooltip cover the next focused control. | Meets |  | There are no arrows to flip. Flip-dependent styling exists as a JS flag: BubbleMenu sets `data-flipped` (`ui/BubbleMenu.jsx:41-51`, styled at `styles.css:430`) and list popovers get `.up` (`ui/popover.js:65`, `styles.css:289`). The mention card (`role="tooltip"`, `ui/MentionCard.jsx:163`) flips above or below (`:92-101`) and closes on blur and on Esc (`:142`, `:115`), so Tab never lands under it. Anchored container queries are beyond our floor anyway. |  |
| 6 | `ui-atoms/pull-to-reveal` | Hide secondary UI above a scroller with `scroll-initial-target` plus snap; always offer a visible button that does the same. | N/A |  | Nothing is hidden above a scroller: list search fields are always visible (`pages/ChatList.jsx:116`); grep `scroll-initial-target\|scroll-snap`: none. |  |
| 7 | `ui-atoms/responsive-table` | Semantic table; wrapper `tabindex=0` and `role=region` with a name; sticky headers; container-query stacked layout when narrow; legible cells. | Partly | Medium | Agent Markdown tables are real `<table>` with GFM `thead`/`th` (`ui/Markdown.jsx:116`; the sanitiser allows table parts, `ui/markdownRules.js:56`) and scroll sideways in `.md-table` (`ui/Markdown.jsx:48-53`, `styles.css:630-633`). The wrapper has no `tabIndex`, role or name. In Safari, and in the iOS shell with a hardware keyboard, keyboard users cannot scroll a wide table; in Chrome, focus lands on an unnamed scroller. There is no sticky header, which is not needed because tables are never height-capped. | In `Table` (`app/web/src/ui/Markdown.jsx:48`), render `<div className="md-table" tabIndex={0} role="region" aria-label="表格">`. Optionally do this only while `scrollWidth > clientWidth` (checked in a ResizeObserver), so small tables add no tab stop. Add `.md-table:focus-visible { outline-offset: -2px }`. Keep sideways scrolling rather than a stacked layout: Agent tables have arbitrary columns and no labels to inject. |
| 8 | `ui-atoms/scroll-position-aware-elements` | Floating back-to-top or scroll-to-bottom controls that appear only after the user scrolls; move focus when they hide; pad content so they don't cover it. | Partly | Medium | The chat tracks whether it is at the newest message (`pages/ChatView.jsx:111`, `:405-424`). Only a detached search or jump window shows 回到最新消息 (`ChatView.jsx:525-538`). After an ordinary scroll-up there is no control to jump to the latest message, and nothing says that new messages arrived below (auto-follow stops, `:283`; mark-read waits, `:452`). | Add a floating 回到最新 button, with the conversation's unread count, inside `.messages`. Show it when `atBottom` turns false: promote the ref to state on change, or use an IntersectionObserver sentinel after the last `<li>` (the guide's fallback; `container-type: scroll-state` is Chromium-only). On click, scroll to the end and focus the list. If it hides while focused, move focus to the list. Add bottom padding to `.messages` (`styles.css:387`) equal to the button's height. |
| 9 | `ui-atoms/scroll-progress-indicator` | Scroll-driven progress bar with feature detection and reduced-motion handling. | N/A |  | No reading-progress UI; grep `animation-timeline\|scroll()`: none. |  |
| 10 | `ui-atoms/scrollability-affordance-hints` | Shadows or fades only where more content exists; optional when the scrollbar shows, required when it's hidden; solid backgrounds can use `background-attachment: local`. | Partly | Low | Desktop scrollbars are visible (thin, styled at `styles.css:31-36`), so hints are optional there. On iPhone and iPad (Safari and the WKWebView shell), scrollbars are overlays that show only while scrolling. The sideways scrollers have no edge cue: Markdown tables `.md-table` (`:630`), code blocks `.md pre` (`:621-622`), and the pill tabs `.pills` (`:134`), which overflow at 320 px with three tabs (`pages/Agents.jsx:311-318`). Dialogs scroll `.modal-body` (`:955`) between a fixed head and foot with no shadow. No `scroll-state` or `animation-timeline` anywhere. | Floor-safe (Safari 16.4) first steps: (1) give `.modal-body` (`styles.css:955`; solid `#fff` from `.modal`, `:948`) the guide's `local` cover plus `scroll` shadow background pair. (2) The sideways scrollers sit on translucent bubbles or the gradient, so use the guide's fade: an IntersectionObserver on edge sentinels toggles `more-start`/`more-end` classes that size a `mask` band. Pseudo-element shadows under `@supports (container-type: scroll-state)` can come later as an enhancement. |
| 11 | `ui-atoms/shrinking-header-on-scroll` | Scroll-linked header shrink, with feature detection, reduced-motion handling and scroll-padding for focus. | N/A |  | Headers are fixed-size flex rows outside the scrollers (`styles.css:349` `.chat-head`); no shrink-on-scroll pattern. |  |
| 12 | `ui-atoms/single-line-overflow` | Keep the full string in the DOM (never cut in script); reveal it on hover and focus-visible plus a touch route; don't rely on `title`; inset outlines inside clipping rows. | Partly | Low | Ellipsis is done right in most places, with `min-width: 0` (`styles.css:162`, `:165`, `:261`, `:302`, `:577`). But: (1) file names are cut in script (`ui/FileCard.jsx:38-63` via `elideHead`, `ui/fileKind.js:77-88`). Screen readers get the full name from an sr-only copy, but copy and selection get the cut text, because the full copy is unselectable (`styles.css:1106`). (2) The full text is revealed only by `title` (`FileCard.jsx:58`; model rows, `pages/ModelSelect.jsx:177`), which touch and keyboard can't reach, so on a phone two long model ids that differ only at the end look the same in the picker sheet. (3) Pill tabs sit in an `overflow-x: auto` row with no top padding (`styles.css:134`) while focus rings sit 2 px outside (`:69`), so the top of the ring is clipped. | (1) Drop the measured cut in `FileName` and rely on the CSS head-ellipsis plus fixed tail that already exists (`styles.css:682-684`), the guide's "keep the end visible" pattern. (2) Under `(hover: none)`, let `.model-option .model-name` wrap (`white-space: normal; overflow-wrap: anywhere`) so the full id is readable in the phone sheet. Hover and keyboard users can keep `title`, or get the guide's scroll-reveal under `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`. (3) Add `.pills button:focus-visible { outline-offset: -2px }`, or 4 px top padding on `.pills`. |
| 13 | `ui-atoms/state-aware-sticky-headers` | Style sticky headers when stuck (scroll-state queries, progressive); set `scroll-padding-top` ≥ the sticky height so focused items stay visible; mind scroll anchoring. | Partly | Low | The only sticky element is the model picker's group label. It is opaque, so it reads fine whether stuck or not (`styles.css:299`), and needs no stuck styling. But `.model-list` (`:298`) has no `scroll-padding-top`. Arrow-key navigation scrolls the active row with `scrollIntoView({block: "nearest"})` (`pages/ModelSelect.jsx:114`), which puts it at the very top, under its group's sticky label (about 30 px tall; rows are 34 px, `:300`). | Add `scroll-padding-top: 30px` (the label's height) to `.model-list` (`app/web/src/styles.css:298`). |
| 14 | `ui-atoms/viewport-aware-dropdowns` | Keep panels tethered to their trigger and flip at viewport edges instead of clipping (anchor positioning plus popover); no ARIA menu roles without the keyboard contract. | Meets |  | The same need is met in JS. `usePopover` places a list below its button, flips it above when room is short, clamps it inline to the window and re-places it on scroll or resize (`ui/popover.js:57-67`, `:87-88`). BubbleMenu and MentionCard flip and clamp the same way (`ui/BubbleMenu.jsx:41-51`, `ui/MentionCard.jsx:92-101`). On phones the lists become bottom sheets (`styles.css:328-339`). They are not in the top layer (portal plus z-index, `styles.css:285`). Native anchor positioning would still need this JS below Safari 26, i.e. across our floor. The 新建 menu claims no `role="menu"` (`pages/ChatList.jsx:104`). |  |

### 1.12 ui-behaviors — 45 guides, 56 rows

Rows: Meets 7 · Partly 22 · Does not meet 5 · N/A 22. Per guide: Meets 2 · Partly 19 · Does not meet 4 · N/A 20.

| # | Guide § rule | What the guidance asks (paraphrased) | Verdict | Sev | Our evidence (file:line) | Change if not met |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `ui-behaviors/2d-panning` | 2D pan surfaces: native scroll container with axis-lock off and overscroll contained; pan via scroll position, not transform; focusable, arrow-key pannable, visible focus. | Partly | Low | Image viewer pans a zoomed picture by its own pointer code with CSS `transform` (`app/web/src/ui/ImageViewer.jsx:146-170`, `:283`), clamped (`app/web/src/ui/imageZoom.js:23-27`); free diagonal movement, no railing; overscroll contained (`app/web/src/styles.css:1099`); `touch-action: none` on the stage because it does its own pinch up to 4x (`styles.css:1114`, `imageZoom.js:5`); keys: arrows step pictures, `+ - 0` zoom about the centre, so a keyboard user cannot pan a zoomed picture (`ImageViewer.jsx:218-231`); every wheel/trackpad scroll zooms, none pans (`ImageViewer.jsx:115-125`); only `cursor: grab`, no grabbing state (`styles.css:1117`). Avatar crop stage is good: focusable group, arrows pan (Shift = bigger), focus ring (`app/web/src/pages/AvatarDialog.jsx:131-145`, `:194-208`, `styles.css:1136-1141`) but its hint does not mention the arrow keys (`AvatarDialog.jsx:193`). | `ImageViewer.jsx` onKeyDown: when `view.scale > 1` make Arrow keys pan through `clampPan` (e.g. 48px, Shift x4) and step pictures only at 1x (or with PageUp/PageDown); in the wheel handler pan by `deltaX/deltaY` when zoomed and not `ctrlKey`, keep ctrl/pinch for zoom; add `.viewer-img[data-dragging]{cursor:grabbing}`. Keep the custom transform pan (native `scroll-axis-lock` is new and not in Safari, and our pinch needs `touch-action:none`). Add 方向键移动 to the hint at `AvatarDialog.jsx:193`. |
| 2 | `ui-behaviors/anchor-positioning-tab-underline` | Animated active-tab indicator via anchor positioning, drawn with a border (survives forced colors), transition under no-preference; always set `aria-current`/`aria-selected`; static border fallback. | Partly | Low | ARIA state is set: rail/tab bar `aria-current="page"` (`app/web/src/AppShell.jsx:43`, `:53`), pills and emoji tabs `aria-selected` (`app/web/src/pages/ChatList.jsx:44`, `app/web/src/pages/Composer.jsx:329`). Indicators are static and drawn with background/colour: rail `.rail-item.active` background (+weight) (`styles.css:103`), phone tab bar colour+weight only (`styles.css:1057`), pills background+weight (`styles.css:139`), emoji tabs background+colour only (`styles.css:771`). No `forced-colors` rule anywhere (searched `forced-colors`): in forced colors the emoji tab selection disappears. No sliding underline (not required). | Add `@media (forced-colors: active) { .rail-item.active, .pills button.active, .emoji-tabs button[aria-selected="true"] { border-block-end: 3px solid Highlight; } }` (or an outline) in `styles.css`. A sliding underline is optional; anchor positioning is not in Safari 16.4, so it must sit behind `@supports (position-anchor: --a)` with the static border as fallback, and its transition under `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`. |
| 3 | `ui-behaviors/animate-element-entry-exit` | Animate elements in with `@starting-style` and out via `transition-behavior: allow-discrete` (separate declaration); delay removal until animations finish; reduced-motion variant. | Partly | Low | Entry only, and only for popups: `@keyframes` run on mount for model/select popover, phone sheet, scrim, bubble menu (`styles.css:287-289`, `:323-333`, `:428-430`), cancelled under reduce (`styles.css:342-345`, `:438`). No `@starting-style`/`allow-discrete` anywhere (searched). Toast (`styles.css:975-978`), emoji/mention pickers (`styles.css:765`), side panel overlay (`styles.css:982`), chat search bar, reply chip appear and vanish instantly; every exit is an instant React unmount. | Optional polish: keep the mount `@keyframes` (works on Safari 16.4; `@starting-style` needs 17.5 and would just not animate there) and add the same short fade-in to `.toast`, `.emoji-picker`, `.mention-picker`, `.side-col` under `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)`. For exits, keep the node mounted with `data-closing` (opacity 0), wait on `el.getAnimations()` (continue at once when it is empty, e.g. under reduced motion) with a ~300ms failsafe, then unmount. |
| 4 | `ui-behaviors/animate-to-from-top-layer` | Animate dialog/popover entry and exit with `@starting-style`, `display`+`overlay` transitions, allow-discrete; animate `::backdrop`; scale only under no-preference; fallback close via `data-closing`. | Partly | Low | No native top-layer elements (searched `<dialog`, `showModal`, `popover`): Modal and image viewer are fixed divs (`app/web/src/ui/primitives.jsx:136-171`, `ImageViewer.jsx:235`). Modal, its backdrop, viewer and mention card have no entry or exit motion (`styles.css:944-951`, `:1093-1099`, `:752-756`); popovers/menus have a mount fade+3px slide, sheet 24px slide (`styles.css:287`, `:333`, `:428`); under reduce the whole animation (fade too) is removed rather than shortened to a fade (`styles.css:342-345`, `:438`). | If dialogs move to native `<dialog>`: `transition-property: opacity, transform, display, overlay; transition-behavior: allow-discrete` (separate line), `@starting-style` inside the open state, scale only under `no-preference`, short fade under `reduce`; Safari/Firefox lack `overlay`, so close through a `data-closing` + `getAnimations()` helper and route `cancel` (Esc) through it. Until then a 120ms fade-in on `.modal-backdrop`/`.modal` under `no-preference` is enough; under `reduce` keep the popovers' fade and drop only the translate. |
| 5 | `ui-behaviors/carousel-snap-highlights` | Style the snapped item of a scroll-snap carousel with `scroll-state(snapped)` queries; never use snapping as selection; keep non-snapped items legible; IntersectionObserver fallback. | N/A |  | No scroll-snap carousel or gallery (searched `scroll-snap`, `snap`, `carousel`); the image viewer steps pictures by buttons, arrow keys and swipe (`ImageViewer.jsx:183-187`, `:309-318`). |  |
| 6 | `ui-behaviors/client-side-routing` | (Guide is frontmatter only.) Description: SPA routing of links and Back/Forward without reloads, with view transitions and correct scroll position, focus and loading feedback. | Partly | Medium | History API routing without reloads: push/replace on each nav (`app/web/src/state/appHistory.js:186-192`), popstate redraws (`appHistory.js:196-204`), auth screens too (`app/web/src/auth/router.js:75-78`); list scroll kept across Back (`AppShell.jsx:139-150`); loading feedback: Suspense fallback (`app/web/src/App.jsx:18`), pending chat shell (`AppShell.jsx:156-170`), `LoadingRow` (`primitives.jsx:177-191`). Gaps: no focus management on a route change (searched every `.focus(`: none on nav) — on a phone the list goes `display:none` (`styles.css:995-996`) and focus drops to `<body>`, Back does not return it to the row; `document.title` never changes (searched); no transition between views; nav entries are buttons, not links (`ChatList.jsx:121-126`, `AppShell.jsx:43`), so no open-in-new-tab. | After a push that changes the visible page, focus the new page's heading (`tabIndex={-1}` on e.g. `.chat-title h2` in `ChatView.jsx:484-485`, the page `h2` elsewhere) with `preventScroll`; on Back/`goUp`, focus the row that opened it (remember the conv/contact/agent id). Set `document.title` per page (e.g. `${convTitle} - Haiyo`). Keep the History API (Navigation API is not in the Safari 16.4 floor). Optional transitions: row 11. |
| 7 | `ui-behaviors/consistent-cross-document-transitions` | Before a cross-document view transition, render-block on critical CSS/JS (`blocking="render"`) and above-the-fold DOM (`<link rel="expect">`); preload morph images; clean temporary names. | N/A |  | One HTML document for the whole site (`app/web/index.html`); the service sends `index.html` for every app path (`app/service/src/web.ts:69`, `:116`); in-site navigations are same-document (`appHistory.js:186-190`); the only full navigations are reloads (`app/web/src/staleChunk.js:10`, `app/web/src/ui/ChunkBoundary.jsx:38`), which cross-document view transitions do not cover. |  |
| 8 | `ui-behaviors/cross-document-transitions` | Opt in with `@view-transition { navigation: auto }` under no-preference on both pages; customise old/new root animations; types via `pagereveal`. | N/A |  | Same as row 7: single-page app, no same-origin cross-document navigations (`app/web/index.html`, `app/service/src/web.ts:116`). |  |
| 9 | `ui-behaviors/custom-button-actions` | Use `commandfor` + `--custom` commands to wire buttons declaratively; manage `aria-pressed`/`aria-expanded`/`aria-haspopup` yourself; `aria-disabled` if disabled on activation. | Partly | Low | Invoker commands not used; React handlers bind each button directly (fine: no polyfill needed, Safari 16.4 lacks the API). ARIA states mostly right: `aria-pressed` on search and details toggles (`app/web/src/pages/ChatView.jsx:500`, `:508`), `aria-expanded` on 新建, 表情, @ (`ChatList.jsx:100`, `Composer.jsx:326`, `:337`), `aria-haspopup` on Select/ModelSelect/picture (`app/web/src/ui/Select.jsx:178`, `app/web/src/pages/ModelSelect.jsx:259`, `app/web/src/pages/Messages.jsx:91`). Missing: 表情 opens a `role="dialog"` (`Composer.jsx:327`) and @ opens a `role="listbox"` (`Composer.jsx:348`) without `aria-haspopup`; the 新建 popup is a role-less `div` with `aria-label` (`ChatList.jsx:104`). Save-while-busy moves focus before disabling (`AvatarDialog.jsx:150-152`). | Add `aria-haspopup="dialog"` to the 表情 button (`Composer.jsx:326`) and `aria-haspopup="listbox"` to the @ button (`Composer.jsx:337`). For 新建 either make it a real menu (`role="menu"`, `role="menuitem"`, arrow keys, `aria-haspopup="menu"`) or keep a disclosure and drop the `aria-label` from the role-less div. |
| 10 | `ui-behaviors/declarative-dialog-popover-control` | Toggle dialogs/popovers with `commandfor`/`command` (`show-modal`, `close`, `toggle-popover`); explicit `type` in forms; return focus to the trigger when closed from inside. | Partly | Low | Opening/closing is React state (native commands do not apply: no `<dialog>`/`popover`). Focus return is mostly handled: dialogs go back to the opener (`app/web/src/ui/dialogFocus.js:41-43`), popovers on Esc (`app/web/src/ui/popover.js:49-52`, `:101-103`), 新建 menu on Esc (`ChatList.jsx:81`). Gaps: the emoji picker's own 关闭表情 button and Esc with focus inside it unmount it with no focus return (`Composer.jsx:328`, `:322`); 发起群聊 is opened from a menu item that unmounts in the same commit (`ChatList.jsx:105`), so the dialog records `<body>` as "where focus was" (`dialogFocus.js:16`) and closing it leaves focus on `<body>`. All `Btn`/`IconBtn` set `type="button"` (`primitives.jsx:103`, `:120`). | Emoji picker: on 关闭表情 and on Esc from inside, focus the 表情 trigger (ref on `Composer.jsx:326`). Give `Modal` a `returnTo` prop passed to `useDialogFocus` (it already supports one, `dialogFocus.js:11`) and pass the 新建 button from `ChatList.jsx` to `NewGroupModal`. |
| 11 | `ui-behaviors/directional-navigation-transitions` | SPA view changes use `startViewTransition({update, types:[forward/backward]})`, `:active-view-transition-type()` slides with transform and easing, reduced-motion off, feature-detect fallback. | Does not meet | Low | No view transitions anywhere (searched `startViewTransition`, `view-transition`); on a phone list↔page is an instant `display` swap (`AppShell.jsx:233`, `styles.css:995-996`). The iOS app plans to turn on edge swipe-back (`docs/design/2026-11-24-store-app-draft.md:157`), which animates on its own. | Phone layout only: wrap the nav dispatch in `document.startViewTransition({ update: () => flushSync(() => dispatch(a)), types: [push ? "forward" : "backward"] })` when `document.startViewTransition` exists (Safari 18+, types 18.2+; on 16.4–17 just dispatch); skip when the popstate came from a browser gesture (`event.hasUAVisualTransition` where available) to avoid a double animation after the iOS swipe; slide with `translate` and `ease-in-out`; `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { ::view-transition-group(*) { animation: none } }`. |
| 12 | `ui-behaviors/drag-and-drop` | Reorder a list's real DOM children by pointer (handle, capture, drop marker, FLIP, autoscroll) with a keyboard alternative and one live announcement per move. | N/A |  | No user-reorderable lists (searched `reorder`, `sortable`, `排序`, `上移`); our only drag-and-drop is dropping files onto a chat (`ChatView.jsx:461-477`, outline cue `styles.css:695`), which has picker buttons as the non-drag path (`Composer.jsx:333-334`). |  |
| 13 | `ui-behaviors/drag` | Drag a floating element by a dedicated focusable handle: primary button, pointer capture, offset kept, clamped; `touch-action`/`user-select` on handle; arrow keys; single-pointer alternative; announce moves. | Partly | Low | No movable floating panels; the guide's handle rules applied to the composer's resize handle: focusable `role="separator"` with `aria-valuenow`, Arrow/Home/End keys, primary button only, pointer capture, start height kept, `cursor: ns-resize`, `touch-action: none` on the handle only (`Composer.jsx:341-347`, `styles.css:708-711`). Gaps: no `user-select: none` on it (relies on `preventDefault` in pointerdown); no `lostpointercapture` cleanup; an 8px-tall target (`styles.css:708`, WCAG 2.5.8 wants 24px); the only non-drag way to resize by pointer is a double-click reset (`Composer.jsx:346`), and the box has a fixed height (`Composer.jsx:70`, `:363`), so a touch user who cannot drag cannot enlarge it (WCAG 2.5.7). | `Composer.jsx:341-347` / `styles.css:708`: add `-webkit-user-select:none; user-select:none`, an `onLostPointerCapture` that clears `drag.current`, and a larger hit area on coarse pointers (e.g. `::before` extending 8px above and below). Provide a non-drag path: auto-grow the textarea with its content up to `maxHeight()` (JS autosize; `field-sizing: content` is not in Safari 16.4). |
| 14 | `ui-behaviors/dynamic-sibling-animations` | Stagger sibling animations with `sibling-index()` behind `@supports` (literal time), JS or `:nth-child` fallback; disable under reduced motion. | Meets |  | The only stagger is the three typing dots: fixed `:nth-child` delays (`styles.css:587-589`) on a fixed three-item element (`app/web/src/pages/AgentChatBits.jsx:65-71`), disabled under reduce (`styles.css:591`). `:nth-child` is the guide's own choice for a small fixed count, and `sibling-index()` is not in the Safari 16.4 floor. |  |

Polish only. | For user-caused changes (switching filter pills, leaving/deleting a chat, removing a member), not for every incoming message: give rows `view-transition-name: conv-<id>` and `view-transition-class: chat-row` (Safari 18.2+), `:root { view-transition-name: none }`, `::view-transition { pointer-events: none }`, wrap the update in `startViewTransition` + `flushSync` when available, skip above ~200 rows, and turn all groups off under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`. |

Esc conflict: the Esc listener is a native listener on the dialog box that calls `stopPropagation` and closes before any React handler inside can react, so Esc in the autofocused `type="search"` box of 发起群聊 / 添加成员 (`app/web/src/pages/GroupModals.jsx:19`, `:73`, `:133`) or the provider model-sync search (`app/web/src/pages/Providers.jsx:559`) closes the whole dialog instead of clearing the field, dropping the typed group name and picked friends. | `dialogFocus.js:20`: do not close when the Esc belongs to a control — e.g. `if (e.target.matches('input[type="search"]') && e.target.value) return;` and `if (e.defaultPrevented) return;` — or move the Esc handling to a React `onKeyDown` on the box so inner handlers can stop it. Longer term: native `<dialog>` + `showModal()` (Safari 15.4+) for top layer and inert background, with `closedby="any"` (not in Safari) plus the current backdrop handler as fallback. |

### 1.13 ui-components — 28 guides, 74 rows

Rows: Meets 11 · Partly 41 · Does not meet 6 · N/A 16. Per guide: Meets 1 · Partly 15 · Does not meet 0 · N/A 12.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| 74 | `toolbar §targets-and-visible-states` | Buttons at least 24×24 via min sizes, larger under `pointer: coarse`; visible focus; hover in `(hover: hover)`; pressed state 3:1 or an extra cue; forced-colors system colours. | Partly | Low | Focus outline global (`app/web/src/styles.css:69`); hover wrapped in `(hover: hover)` (`app/web/src/styles.css:229`); composer tools are fixed 30×30 at every pointer (`app/web/src/styles.css:712-713`; not enlarged in the ≤860px block `:986-1039`), small for touch; pressed state is a pale fill only (`--purple-soft`, `app/web/src/styles.css:234`), lost in forced colors (no rule) | Under `@media (pointer: coarse)` give the composer tool buttons `min-width: 44px; min-height: 44px` (min-, not width/height); give `.icon-btn[aria-pressed="true"]` an inset border or outline meeting 3:1, plus a forced-colors rule using `Highlight`. |

### 1.14 user-experience — 5 guides, 21 rows

Rows: Meets 6 · Partly 10 · Does not meet 5 · N/A 0. Per guide: Meets 0 · Partly 5 · Does not meet 0 · N/A 0.

| # | Guide § rule | What the guidance asks (paraphrased) | Verdict | Sev | Our evidence (file:line) | Change if not met |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `user-experience/content-first-noise-reduction §no-interruptions` | No modals or interstitials on load; required notices as small non-modal bars; blocking dialogs only for consequential actions. | Meets |  | No dialog opens unasked; modals come from user actions (`app/web/src/ui/primitives.jsx:136-171`). Feedback is a non-modal toast (`app/web/src/AppShell.jsx:240-244`) or an inline notice. No consent banner is present. |  |
| 2 | `user-experience/content-first-noise-reduction §stable-content` | Don't move content while people read: reserve space for images and embeds, don't insert above what's on screen, no popovers they didn't ask for. | Meets |  | Pictures reserve their box with an `aspect-ratio` set from their size (`app/web/src/pages/Messages.jsx:93`, `styles.css:658-660`). Avatars are sized inline (`primitives.jsx:27-28`). Mention cards are fixed-position, so they never move text (`styles.css:751-753`). |  |
| 3 | `user-experience/content-first-noise-reduction §compact-bars` | Keep each fixed or sticky bar under 12% of viewport height and to one row; don't stack bars; move extras into menus. | Partly | Low | The phone tab bar (50px plus inset, `styles.css:1046-1054`) and the one-row chat header (`:999-1008`) are compact. Above the messages, though, the search bar, the block notice and the two-button 正在查看较早的消息 notice can stack (`app/web/src/pages/ChatView.jsx:512-538`), and the composer sits below (30px tools + 64px field + padding, `styles.css:707-719`, `:1016`). | Turn the "viewing older messages" notice into a small floating 回到最新 pill at the bottom edge of the list, and keep at most one bar above the list. |
| 4 | `user-experience/content-first-noise-reduction §hide-bars-on-scroll` | Hide top and bottom bars while scrolling down and bring them back on scroll up (scroll-state query, JS fallback); keep them while they hold focus. | Does not meet | Low | The header and tab bar are always shown: no scroll-state queries (grep `container-type`) and no scroll-direction script. | Optional for a chat UI. On phone list pages, hide the tab bar while `.rows` scrolls down. Scroll-state queries are Chromium-only, so Safari and the WKWebView need the guide's small JS fallback. Keep the bar shown on `:focus-within`. |
| 5 | `user-experience/content-first-noise-reduction §popover-and-details` | Put secondary UI in `popover` menus or drawers and in `details`, not in permanent toolbars or custom JS overlays. | Partly | Low | Secondary UI is on demand: menus, the model picker, the details panel and `<details class="thinking">` (`app/web/src/pages/AgentChatBits.jsx:55`, `:181`). But every popup is a custom JS overlay (`app/web/src/ui/popover.js`, `BubbleMenu.jsx`, `MentionCard.jsx`), not `popover`. `popover` needs Safari 17, above our 16.4 floor. | When the floor reaches Safari 17, move `.menu`, `.select-pop` and `.bubble-menu` to `popover="auto"` (top layer, light dismiss and Esc built in). Until then keep popover.js, which already closes on Esc and outside press (`popover.js:102-107`), or load the popover polyfill conditionally. |
| 6 | `user-experience/form-factor-adaptation §target-size` | Make controls ≥24×24px, and ≥44×44 under `(pointer: coarse)`. Widen small icons with a pseudo-element hit area. Links inside sentences are exempt. | Partly | Medium | Input queries exist (`styles.css:63`, `:222`, `:418`, `:629`), but only `.text-link` grows for touch (`:222`). Under 24px with any pointer: `.msg-action` 20px (`:408`), button `.tag` 21px (`:185`, `:196`), `.whisper-hit-more`/`.whisper-fold` ≈18px (`:474`, `:486`), `.msg-lookups > button` ≈18px (`:541`), `.composer-resize` 8px (`:708`). Under 44px on touch: composer tools 30px (`:713`), `.reply-chip .icon-btn` 26px (`:791`), chat-search close 28px (`:361`), emoji close 24px (`:768`), modal close 38px (`:226`, `:953`), `.back` ≈35px (`:795`), `.pills button` 34px (`:136`), `.btn` 38px and `.btn-sm` 30px (`:203`, `:210`), `.emoji-grid button` 36px (`:773`), `.menu button` ≈37px (`:242`), `.model-option` 34px at 481–860px (`:300`, `:339`). Already fine: tab bar (`:1054`), chat-head icons (`:1000`), bubble menu (`:432`), viewer (`:1111`). | Add `:root { --target: 24px } @media (pointer: coarse) { :root { --target: 44px } }`. Apply `min-block-size` and `min-inline-size: var(--target)` to `button, [role=button], .icon-btn, .btn, .pills button, .back, .menu button, .model-option, .select-option`. For icons that should look small (`.composer-tools`, `.reply-chip`, the `.chat-search` close), keep the drawing and add `position: relative` with `::after { content: ""; position: absolute; inset: -8px }`. Make the resize grip ≥24px tall; its visible line can stay 2px. |
| 7 | `user-experience/form-factor-adaptation §container-queries` | Lay out components with container queries; keep media queries for input type and user settings. | Does not meet | Low | No `@container` or `container-type` (grep). Component rules follow the viewport: `.file-card` wraps when the viewport is ≤480px (`styles.css:687`), and pictures are sized from `100vw` (`:662`), even though the message column also narrows when the 348px side panel opens (`:116`, `:982`). | Add `.messages { container: chat / inline-size }`. Move the `.file-card` wrap, the `.attachment-image` max width (in `cqi` instead of `vw`) and the `.msg-main` width into `@container chat (…)`. Container queries are Safari 16+, inside our floor. |
| 8 | `user-experience/form-factor-adaptation §safe-areas` | With `viewport-fit=cover`, pad fixed and edge-anchored UI by every safe-area inset; add `scroll-padding` so focused items aren't hidden under fixed bars. | Partly | Low | `viewport-fit=cover` is set (`index.html:6`). The shell pads top, right and left (`styles.css:79-82`), and each bottom-edge container pads the bottom (`:85`, `:88`, `:348`, `:805`, `:909`, `:1050`). Modal and auth layers pad all four sides (`:946`, `:1069`), as do the viewer bar and stage (`:1102`, `:1114`). The tab bar is a flex sibling, so nothing scrolls under it. Gaps: JS-placed popups clamp to a fixed 8–12px window edge, not the insets (`app/web/src/ui/popover.js:7`, `:61-67`; `app/web/src/ui/MentionCard.jsx:18`, `:99-100`). The sticky `.model-group-label` (`styles.css:299`) can cover an option scrolled into view with `block: "nearest"` (`app/web/src/pages/ModelSelect.jsx:114`). | In popover.js and MentionCard, read the insets once (e.g. from a hidden probe with `padding: env(safe-area-inset-*)`) and clamp to `max(EDGE, inset)`. Add `.model-list { scroll-padding-top: 2rem }`. |
| 9 | `user-experience/form-factor-adaptation §balance-and-pretty` | `text-wrap: balance` on short headings and `pretty` on body text, not on `*`. | Does not meet | Low | None used (grep). | See row 33. |
| 10 | `user-experience/form-factor-adaptation §icon-button-names` | Give every icon-only button an accessible name, and hide decorative icons with `aria-hidden`. | Meets |  | `IconBtn` sets `aria-label` and `title` (`app/web/src/ui/primitives.jsx:118-123`). The one raw icon-only button is labelled (`app/web/src/pages/MemoryRoom.jsx:152-153`). A multiline grep for icon-only `<button>` found no unlabelled ones. |  |
| 11 | `user-experience/form-factor-adaptation §content-inside-its-box` | Nothing clipped or overflowing down to 320px. Wide content scrolls in its own focusable, named region. Don't ellipsis-truncate headings or set `overflow-x: hidden` on html/body. | Partly | Medium | Mostly done: `min-width: 320px` (`styles.css:42`), `min-width: 0` on flex items throughout, Markdown wraps and scrolls sideways (`:596`, `:622`, `:630`, `:637`), and nothing hides overflow on html or body. Gaps: `.md-table` and `pre` scroll but are neither focusable nor named (`app/web/src/ui/Markdown.jsx:48-53`, `:74-75`), so in Safari a keyboard user can't scroll a wide table or code block. The chat title `h2` is cut with an ellipsis on wide screens and clamped to two lines on phones (`styles.css:351`, `:1008`). | Give `.md-table` and `pre` `tabIndex={0}`, `role="region"`, `aria-label="表格"`/`"代码"` and a focus ring. Let `.chat-title h2` wrap to two lines on desktop too, with the full title in a `title` attribute. |
| 12 | `user-experience/natural-interactions §hover-press-focus` | Give hover, press and keyboard focus distinct looks. The `:focus-visible` ring needs ≥3:1; remove outlines only for an equally visible replacement. | Partly | Medium | Hover tints apply only under `(hover: hover)` (e.g. `styles.css:102`, `:209`, `:229`). The global `:focus-visible` ring is 2px purple, 5.2:1 on the page (`:69`). There is no press state: no `:active` rules apart from a cursor (`:1140`), so touch relies on WebKit's default tap highlight. Text fields and triggers replace the outline with a 1px border-colour change (`:267-268`, `:516`, `:739`, `:874`). `.chat-search input` removes its outline (`:358`) and `.chat-search` has no `:focus-within` style (`:354-361`), so that field shows no focus at all. | Add `.chat-search:focus-within { border-color: var(--purple) }`, as `.search` has (`:133`). Give fields a 2px ring (`box-shadow: 0 0 0 2px var(--purple)` plus `outline: 2px solid transparent`). Add a press state, e.g. `.btn:active, .icon-btn:active, .row:active { background: var(--purple-tint) }` or `scale: 0.97`. |
| 13 | `user-experience/natural-interactions §open-from-trigger` | Popovers and dropdowns should visibly open from their trigger, on the compositor, respecting reduced motion. | Meets |  | Popups slide 3px out from the trigger's side and flip with their placement (`styles.css:287-289`, `:323-324`, `:428-430`); phone sheets rise from the bottom (`:325`, `:333`). All of it is off under reduced motion (`:342-345`, `:438`). JS placement (`app/web/src/ui/popover.js:61-67`) is the guide's own fallback where anchor positioning is missing, as in Safari 16.4. The optional motion blur isn't used. |  |
| 14 | `user-experience/natural-interactions §swipeable-rows` | Horizontal rows (chips, galleries) scroll natively and snap with `scroll-snap-type` and `scroll-snap-align`; the row is focusable if its items aren't. | Partly | Low | The filter pills are a native sideways scroller with focusable buttons (`styles.css:134-139`), but they don't snap. | Add `.pills { scroll-snap-type: x proximity } .pills button { scroll-snap-align: start }`; proximity, so short rows don't jump. |
| 15 | `user-experience/natural-interactions §keyboard-reachable` | Use `<button>` and `<a href>`. Menus open by click or keyboard and close on Esc. On touch the long-press menu replaces it; it also opens with ContextMenu or Shift+F10 (`app/web/src/ui/BubbleMenu.jsx:252`) and closes on Esc (`:99`). Popovers close on Esc or an outside press (`popover.js:102-107`). `.md-copy` is always visible under `(hover: none)` (`styles.css:629`). One small gap: the composer's resize line is drawn only on hover or focus (`:709-711`). |  |
| 16 | `user-experience/guided-navigation` (frontmatter only; judged on its description) | Move between views fast, without full reloads; keep scroll and input state; use directional transitions (view transitions, speculation rules). | Partly | Low | The SPA uses pushState/replaceState, with no reloads (`app/web/src/state/appHistory.js:106`, `:189`). ### 1.15 visual-design — 27 guides, 53 rows

Rows: Meets 6 · Partly 14 · Does not meet 16 · N/A 17. Per guide: Meets 1 · Partly 8 · Does not meet 3 · N/A 15.

| # | Guide § rule | What the guidance asks (paraphrased) | Verdict | Sev | Our evidence (file:line) | Change if not met |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `visual-design/adapt-scrollbar-to-contrast-preferences` | Under `prefers-contrast: more`, override scrollbar thumb/track with stark colours; the default thumb must already reach 3:1 against its track. | Does not meet | Low | No `prefers-contrast` anywhere in `app/web` (grep); thumb `#b6a7e4` on a transparent track (`app/web/src/styles.css:31`, `:34`, `:732`, `:736`) measures 1.6–2.2:1 against the surfaces behind it. | After fixing the default thumb (row 21), add `@media (prefers-contrast: more) { :root { --scrollbar-thumb: #1d1b2e; --scrollbar-track: #fff } }` and make the `*`, composer and picker scrollbar rules (`:31`, `:732`, `:772`, `:776`) read those tokens. |
| 2 | `visual-design/apply-webgl-shaders` | Apply WebGL shaders to live HTML through HTML-in-Canvas (`content="drawable"`, texture upload, geometry sync), with a support check and an overlay fallback. | N/A |  | No WebGL/WebGPU: grep `getContext(` finds only 2D contexts (`app/web/src/ui/avatarImage.js:111`, `:125`, `:138`; `app/web/src/ui/FileCard.jsx:24`). |  |

| 4 | `visual-design/browser-ui-theming §no-user-select-none-on-content` | Don't put `user-select: none` on content text, because it breaks copy, translation and "read from here". Thumb contrast is 1.6–2.2:1. | Remove `scrollbar-width: thin` (leaving `auto`) and the 7px `::-webkit-scrollbar` widths; darken the thumb (row 21). |
| 6 | `visual-design/browser-ui-theming §validity-styling` | Use `:user-invalid`/`:user-valid`, not `:invalid`, so empty required fields aren't flagged on load. | Meets |  | No `:invalid`/`:valid` selectors (grep). | 7 | `visual-design/browser-ui-theming §text-fields` | Style inputs and textareas as normal boxes; use `resize: vertical` and never `resize: none` without another resize mechanism. `field-sizing` is optional. | Meets |  | `.field textarea { resize: vertical }` (`styles.css:873`). The composer uses `resize: none` (`:730`), but a keyboard-operable resize separator replaces it (`app/web/src/pages/Composer.jsx:341-347`, `styles.css:708`). |  |
| 8 | `visual-design/browser-ui-theming §choice-controls` | For dropdowns use native `<select>` with `appearance: base-select`; put inline choices inside `<label>`; use `<button>`, never `<input type=button>`. | Partly | Low | Inline choices meet this: inputs sit inside labels (`.check`, `.checks label`, `.radio-cards label`, `styles.css:854-889`; Switch at `primitives.jsx:209-211`), and grep finds no `<input type="button">`. Dropdowns are a rebuilt combobox/listbox with hand-written keyboard and AT behaviour (`app/web/src/ui/Select.jsx:144-181`, `app/web/src/pages/ModelSelect.jsx`). | For the plain `Select.jsx` choices (no search, no marks), consider a native `<select>`. base-select is Chromium-only, so Safari, iOS and the WKWebView would show the OS picker, which is the better phone UI. | 12 | `visual-design/color §prefers-contrast` | Use `@media (prefers-contrast: more)` to strengthen subtle borders and muted text, once the defaults already pass. | Does not meet | Low | No `prefers-contrast` (grep). ## Part 2 — Sizes and devices the app has not been adapted to

### 2.1 Facts, with sources

Apple's design guidance (all read 2026-11-23; HIG pages were read through their JSON form at `developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/design/human-interface-guidelines/<page>.json`, which carries the same text):

- **Designing for iPhone Duo** (new page, 9 Sept 2026) — https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/designing-for-iphone-duo
  - Two displays, each with a front camera, and a centre hinge; many poses (book, flat, tent, standing). Don't design a layout per pose: use size classes — **compact width on the outer display, regular width on the inner one** — and let the existing layout expand.
  - Build to resize; use layout margins and safe-area insets; avoid fixed widths and display-specific dependencies. Keep the same functions and state on both displays; on the inner display it is fine to show one more level of hierarchy (Mail: list *or* message when closed, both side by side when open).
  - The outer display is **wider and shorter** than other iPhones, so the system moves toolbars, tab bars and the status bar to **the side**; on the inner display they stay on the side in landscape. Content space is therefore **asymmetric**: use safe areas, including for controls on the opposite edge when two apps share the inner display (Split View multitasking; each app puts its controls on its outer edge).
  - **Reserved regions**: the outer camera (always), the inner camera (only while active), and the **folding region** (only when partly open, dividing the inner display). System split views, sheets, alerts and menus adapt to the fold; Notes makes its two panes equal when folded. Avoid extreme layout changes when folding; prefer small adjustments.
- **Preparing your app for iPhone Duo** (developer article) — https://developer.apple.com/documentation/technologyoverviews/preparing-your-app-for-iphone-duo — size views to their container, not to fixed iPhone sizes; base layout on the scene's bounds and size classes, never on device idiom or orientation; apps built with Xcode 26 or earlier don't extend under the status bar and camera.
- **UIView.ReservedRegion** — https://developer.apple.com/documentation/uikit/uiview/reservedregion — `reservedRegions(kind:)` returns *occlusions* (cameras) and *divisions* (the fold) in view coordinates; a division is active only when the device is partly open; scrolling containers don't split their content at a division by themselves.
- **Layout** (updated 9 Sept 2026) — https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/layout — size classes describe the space available on each axis (compact/regular width, compact/regular height); **decide layout by size class, not by device type or orientation**; consider every combination; keep functions the same while showing more or less of them (e.g. tab bar → sidebar with more room). The current page no longer has the device-size tables.
- **Layout, archived copy of 6 Oct 2025** (JSON at https://web.archive.org/web/20251006040430/https://developer.apple.com/tutorials/data/design/human-interface-guidelines/layout.json) — the device tables used below: iPad points **744×1133** (mini 8.3"), **768×1024** (9.7", mini 7.9"), **810×1080** (10.2"), **820×1180** (iPad 11", Air 11"/10.9"), **834×1194** (Pro 11"), **1024×1366** (12.9"/13"); every iPad full screen is **regular width and regular height in both orientations**; iPhones are compact width in portrait, and in landscape compact height (Plus/Max/Air: regular width, others compact width). Its iPadOS section: windows resize freely down to a minimum; **defer switching to a compact layout as long as possible**; test halves, thirds and quarters; consider a tab bar that converts to a sidebar.
- **Designing for iPadOS** — https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/designing-for-ipados — touch, keyboard, trackpad and Pencil, often combined; adapt to orientation, multitasking, Dark Mode and Dynamic Type.
- **Multitasking** (HIG) — https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/multitasking — iPad runs apps full screen or **windowed** (resizable, tiled); apps get no signal about the configuration, so they must adapt to any size. **Windows** (HIG) — https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/windows — windowed apps get **window controls at the top-leading corner**; move leading controls inward when they appear.
- **Multitask on iPad** (Apple Support, iPadOS 27) — https://support.apple.com/en-us/125309 — Windowed Apps, two windows side by side with a draggable divider, and **Slide Over again from iPadOS 26.2, resizable**. Apple does not publish fixed Split View or Slide Over widths any more (the archived article https://support.apple.com/en-us/102576 describes the old Split View/Slide Over and is marked no longer updated).
- **UIView.LayoutRegion** (iOS/iPadOS 26) — https://developer.apple.com/documentation/uikit/uiview/layoutregion — `safeArea(cornerAdaptation:)`, `margins(cornerAdaptation:)` and `layoutGuide(for:)`: the UIKit way to keep clear of corner elements such as the iPad window controls.
- **iPhone Duo tech specs** — https://www.apple.com/iphone-duo/specs/ — outer display 5.4", **1398×2034 px at 460 ppi**; inner display 7.6" folding, **1878×2670 px at 430 ppi**; Apple Pencil (USB-C) supported.

What WebKit/Safari expose to web content:

- **WebKit Features for Safari 27.1** (23 Oct 2026) — https://webkit.org/blog/18486/webkit-features-for-safari-27-1/
  - Same engine as every iPhone; Safari's viewport on iPhone Duo ranges **382–951 CSS px wide and 368–902 tall**: outer portrait 382 wide (svh 580 / lvh 618), outer landscape 594 (368/406), inner portrait 669 (867/902), inner landscape 951 (585/620), each of two side-by-side windows 385 (571/609).
  - **Device Posture API** (`@media (device-posture: folded)`, `navigator.devicePosture`) and **Viewport Segments API** (`horizontal-viewport-segments` / `vertical-viewport-segments` media features, `env(viewport-segment-width|height|top|right|bottom|left <x> <y>)`, `window.viewport.segments`) exist in Safari 27.1 **only behind feature flags** (Settings › Apps › Safari › Advanced › Feature Flags). Off by default.
  - In Safari, page content does **not** extend under the side controls of the outer display; Safari fills that strip with the page's background colour. WebKit is still deciding how pages may use it (they note many sites inset only some edges).
- **Apple Developer Forums, "Adapting WKWebView content to reserved regions on iPhone Duo"** (Apple Frameworks Engineer, 23 Sept 2026) — https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/848036 — both APIs are opt-in experiments in Safari and **cannot be enabled in WKWebView**; the recommended route is for the UIKit host to pass the geometry to the page; **CSS safe-area insets are not affected by the fold**.
- Not found anywhere above: the fold's width, the inner display's points-per-pixel scale, the minimum iPad window size, and whether WKWebView reports the iPad window controls as a safe-area inset (see "Could not verify").

Measured on the iPhone Duo simulator (iOS 27.2, the current shell build; reported to me, not re-run):

- Outer display, closed: the web view is **466×678 pt**. Signed out and signed in the page stays clear of the camera and the status column on the **right** (the column starts at about 390 pt, so the right inset is about 76 pt — derived, not read from `env()`). Signed in: the narrow layout (bottom tab bar, list, chat); a long chat title is shortened.
- Inner display, open and half-open (130°): **951×669 pt**, filled without black bars although the shell declares iPhone-only and portrait-only. Signed in: the wide layout (rail, list and chat side by side), clear of the status column; half-open looks the same as open — nothing avoids the fold.
- Closed → half-open → open → closed (also with a chat open) kept the page and the selected chat, with no reload.
- Defects seen at 951 pt: the chat header has no solid background, so the line under the first visible message shows through between the avatars and the title; the file card squeezes (name cut to "sd1…0991_2.mp4", 「仅本会话可见」 wraps).
- iPad, iPhone build in compatibility mode: the iPadOS window controls (•••) overlap the top-left corner, likely over a page's top-left back button. iPad signed in: not observed.
- Shell's native cannot-connect screen: top and bottom pinned to the safe area, leading and trailing to the view edges — on the outer display its right edge reaches the status column.

Derived: 1398×2034 px ÷ 3 = **466×678 pt**, exactly the measured outer size. The inner display's 951×669 pt matches WebKit's 951 (landscape) and 669 (portrait) widths, but Apple's 1878×2670 px implies about 2.81 px per point, a scale Apple does not publish: the inner point size rests on the measurement and WebKit's table, not on an Apple spec. The fold is assumed to be at the centre of the inner display (x ≈ 475.5 pt in the 951 pt web view); its width is not published.

### 2.4 Where safe areas are assumed symmetric or ignored

Handled correctly (keep): `.app` top, left and right (`styles.css:81`); every scroller's, the composer's and the tab bar's bottom (`:85, :88, :348, :805, :909, :1050`); modal backdrop on all four sides with a 20 px minimum (`:946, :949`); sign-in pages on all four sides (`:1069`); image viewer bar and stage (`:1102, :1114, :1128-1129`); details overlay top and right (`:982`).

Ignored or symmetric — each reaches under the Duo's right-hand status column, and under a left notch in landscape:

1. **Toast** (`styles.css:975-978`): `left: 50%` of the full viewport and `max-width: min(560px, calc(100vw - 32px))`. With a 76 px right inset it is centred 38 px off and any toast wider than ~314 px runs under the column. Change: `left: calc(50% + (env(safe-area-inset-left) - env(safe-area-inset-right)) / 2)` and subtract both insets in `max-width`.
2. **Phone picker sheets** `.model-pop, .select-pop` at ≤480 (`:330-334`): `left: 0; right: 0` with 10 px side padding. Change: side padding `calc(10px + env(safe-area-inset-left|right))`.
3. **Details overlay** `.side-col`: `left: var(--rail)` at 641–860 (`:997`) and `left: 0` at ≤640 (`:1061`) ignore the left inset (an absolute box is placed from the padding edge, so `.app`'s padding does not help). Change: add `env(safe-area-inset-left)` to both.
4. **JS-placed popups** clamp to `window.innerWidth/innerHeight` minus 8–12 px: `ui/popover.js:61-67, 75`, `ui/MentionCard.jsx:99-100`, `ui/BubbleMenu.jsx:186` (when there is no list rect). Change: clamp to the inset box (read the four insets from one hidden probe element styled with `padding: env(safe-area-inset-*)`; the size-class code in 2.7 needs the same values).
5. **Picture sizes from the window**: `.attachment-image` `calc(0.574 * (100vw - 24px))` (`:662`) and `thumbMaxWidth(window.innerWidth)` (`pages/Messages.jsx:77`, `ui/thumbSize.js:10-13`) count the insets and, at 641–860, the rail. Change: size from the pane (`cqi` units / the pane's width).
6. `.mention-card` `max-width: calc(100vw - 16px)` (`:753`) and `.emoji-picker` `calc(100vw - 100px)` (`:766`): same pattern, low impact.
7. **Shell offline screen** (`app/ios/Haiyo/OfflineView.swift:59-60`, branch `origin/lane/mac-app-shell`): leading/trailing pinned to the view edges. Change: pin them to `safeAreaLayoutGuide` (measured defect).
8. **The fold is not a safe area** (Apple forum): nothing in CSS insets will ever keep content off it; it needs the shell's report (2.7, 2.8).

### 2.5 Touch versus pointer assumptions

- `(pointer: coarse)` is used as "touch, so no keyboard and no hover". An iPad with a Magic Keyboard or trackpad is likely still coarse: the hover-only 回复 action stays hidden (`styles.css:418-424`; right-click still opens the bubble menu, `ui/BubbleMenu.jsx:237`, good) and the "Enter 发送 · Shift+Enter 换行" and "↑↓ 选择 · Enter 确认" hints are hidden although Enter works (`pages/Composer.jsx:349, 380`). Change: hide hover-only actions under `(hover: none)`; show keyboard hints under `(any-pointer: fine)` or after the first hardware key press. Keep the 16 px field rule under `(pointer: coarse)` — it is what stops iOS zooming.
- Message text cannot be selected on any touch screen (`:423`) — on a 7.6" or iPad screen, people expect to select text (Part 1 rows).
- The composer's resize grip is an 8 px drag strip (`:708`) — usable with a mouse, hardly with a finger or Pencil (Part 1).
- `(max-width: 480px)` doubling as "touch" (`:222` 44 px links; sheets) — harmless today, but the sheet decision should follow the width class, not a device guess.
- Good: hover effects are fenced behind `(hover: hover)`; no orientation queries; no user-agent sniffing (iPadOS Safari presents itself as a Mac by default, so sniffing would misfire).

### 2.6 Proposed rule: two size classes from the space available

**The rule.** Measure the space the page actually has — the layout viewport minus the safe-area insets on each side (not minus the keyboard; the visual-viewport code keeps handling that) — and classify each axis:

| Axis | Compact | Regular |
|---|---|---|
| Width (available) | < 720 px | ≥ 720 px |
| Height (available) | < 500 px | ≥ 500 px |

- **Panes** follow the width class: compact → one pane at a time (the list, or the page pushed over it with a back button); regular → list and page side by side (list `clamp(280px, 32%, 360px)`). The details panel docks as a third column only when ≥ 1280 px are available; otherwise it is a sheet over the page with a scrim (Esc, focus moved in, the page behind inert).
- **Navigation** is a bottom tab bar only when width is compact **and** height is regular; in every other case a side rail on the leading edge (in compact height: icons only, no brand mark or avatar, scrollable).
- **Inside a pane**, components adapt to that pane's width with container queries; no component rule reads the window.
- **Fold**: when a vertical fold is reported and width is regular, the rail and list fill the segment before the fold and the page starts after it; when a horizontal fold is reported, centred dialogs and sheets move to the lower segment. Nothing else moves (Apple: small adjustments, not rearrangement).

**Why these numbers.** Two panes need a 280 px list, a conversation no narrower than a phone's (~380 px) and the rail (~60 px): about 720. Below 500 px of height a bottom bar (50 px), a chat header (~66 px) and the composer (~110 px) leave under 275 px for messages, so the bar moves to the side — and all phone-landscape heights (320–440) and the Duo's outer landscape (368–406) fall below 500, matching Apple's compact height for phones in landscape.

**What it gives on each device.**

| Device / window | Available (approx.) | Classes (w × h) | Result | Change from today |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| iPhones, portrait | 320–440 × 520–890 | compact × regular | Bar · 1 pane | none |
| iPhones, landscape (Safari) | 450–840 × 300–420 | compact or regular × compact | Rail · 1 pane (2 panes on Plus/Max) | rail instead of bar; rail never cut off |
| Duo outer, closed | ~390 × ~640 | compact × regular | Bar · 1 pane | none (measured fine) |
| Duo outer, landscape | 594 × 368 | compact × compact | Rail · 1 pane | rail instead of bar |
| Duo inner, open / half-open | ~880 × 669 | regular × regular | Rail · 2 panes, split at the fold when half-open | fold-aware split |
| Duo inner, rotated | 669 × ~950 | compact × regular | Bar · 1 pane | bar instead of a 60 px rail |
| iPad portrait 744–834 | 744–834 × 1100+ | regular × regular | Rail · 2 panes | **two panes instead of one** |
| iPad 1024+ (either orientation) | 1024–1366 | regular × regular | Rail · 2 panes; details docked from 1280 | panes scale |
| iPad windows | any | by size | e.g. half of 13" (~680) → Bar · 1 pane | one switch point instead of two |
| Desktop 720–860 | 720–860 | regular | Rail · 2 panes | two panes instead of one |

Optional later refinement (needs device testing): when one side inset is ≥ 44 px (a system column, as on the Duo's outer display), dock the navigation as a rail on that side instead of a bottom bar, as Apple's own bars do there.

### 2.7 Changes this implies in our CSS and components

1. **One source of truth.** A new `app/web/src/ui/sizeClass.js` replaces `ui/breakpoints.js` and `usePhone`/`PHONE_QUERY` in `ui/pointer.js`: it reads the available width and height (layout viewport minus the insets from a hidden probe element), recomputes on `resize` and on the shell's fold event, writes `data-width`, `data-height`, `data-nav` (bar | rail), `data-details` (docked | sheet) and `data-fold` on `<html>`, and offers `useSizeClass()`. It is JS because media queries cannot subtract `env()` insets, and a size container on `.app` would make it the containing block of every fixed layer.
2. **`styles.css`**: the `max-width: 860px` block (`:986-1039`) becomes `:root[data-width="compact"]`; the 640 block (`:1045-1062`) becomes `:root[data-nav="bar"]`; the 1280 rule (`:981-983`) becomes `:root[data-details="sheet"]`; `.only-mobile` (`:74, :988`) follows the width class; the 480 sheet rules (`:328-341`) follow the width class. `(pointer)`, `(hover)` and `(prefers-reduced-motion)` queries stay.
3. **Fluid panes**: `--list: clamp(280px, 32%, 360px)`, `--side: clamp(300px, 28%, 348px)`; a compact-height rail variant (icons only, `overflow-y: auto`, brand mark and avatar hidden).
4. **Container queries**: `container-type: inline-size` on `.list-col`, `.main-col`, `.side-col` and `.modal`; move the rules that today read the window — file card wrap (`:687`), picture width (`:662`, in `cqi`), `.msg-main` width (`:390, :1012`), chat header compaction (`:999-1008, :1042`), `.kv-row`, `.radio-cards`, `.approvals`, `.profile-head` (`:1021-1038`), model panel (`:827-830`), whisper height (`:490-494`) — into `@container` rules. Give `.chat-head` the pane's surface and a bottom line (measured see-through header). **Prerequisite:** render `Modal`, the sheets and the toast outside the panes — native `<dialog>` with `showModal()` (top layer; Safari 15.4+, inside our floor) or a portal to `<body>` — otherwise a container would trap their fixed backdrops (Part 1, css-html could-not-verify).
5. **JS that reads the window**: `ui/thumbSize.js:10-13` and `pages/Messages.jsx:77` (pane width), `ui/popover.js:22, 61-75`, `ui/MentionCard.jsx:99-100`, `ui/BubbleMenu.jsx:186` (inset box), `pages/Composer.jsx:101` (available height), `AppShell.jsx:180-186, 234, 239` (`phone` → `nav === "bar"`), `pages/ChatView.jsx:103, 502`, `pages/Settings.jsx:52, 73`.
6. **Safe areas**: the seven page-side fixes in 2.4.
7. **Compact-height chrome**: one-line chat header, composer capped lower (e.g. `min(200px, 35dvh)`), list header without the brand name, never a bottom bar.
8. **Fold**: hooks such as `:root[data-fold="vertical"][data-width="regular"] .list-col { width: calc(var(--fold-start) - var(--rail-width) - env(safe-area-inset-left)); }` and a leading pad of `var(--fold-gap)` on `.main-col`, fed by the shell; later, for Safari when unflagged, the same variables from `@media (horizontal-viewport-segments: 2)` with `env(viewport-segment-right 0 0)` and `env(viewport-segment-left 1 0)`.
9. **Pointer**: 2.5's two changes (`(hover: none)` for hiding hover-only actions; keyboard hints under `(any-pointer: fine)`), plus a visible "more" button on bubbles for touch (Part 1 context-menu row).
10. **Tests**: `tests/mobile-styles.test.js` asserts that no `max-width` media query drives the shell layout; unit tests for `sizeClass` (insets subtracted, thresholds, fold); e2e at 466×678 (right inset emulated with a CSS override — Playwright cannot set `env()`), 594×368, 744×1133, 820×1180, 1024×1366, 1366×1024, 680×1024, 951×669, 874×402, 956×440; then a Device Hub pass with the real shell.

### 2.8 The iOS shell (`app/ios` on `origin/lane/mac-app-shell`)

Today:

- Device families: `TARGETED_DEVICE_FAMILY = 1` — iPhone only — for the app (`Haiyo.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:324, 352, 380`) and the tests (`:394, :409, :424`); `SUPPORTS_MACCATALYST = NO`, `SUPPORTS_MAC_DESIGNED_FOR_IPHONE_IPAD = NO` (`:319-320` and the other configurations); deployment target iOS 26.0 (`:258`).
- `Support/Info.plist`: `UISupportedInterfaceOrientations` = portrait only, no `~ipad` key (`:61`); no `UIRequiresFullScreen`; `UIApplicationSupportsMultipleScenes` = false (`:41`); scene lifecycle; `UIUserInterfaceStyle` = Light (`:65`); `LSRequiresIPhoneOS` = true (`:31`).
- `Haiyo/ShellViewController.swift`: the web view is pinned to all four edges of the root view (`:82-88`) with `contentInsetAdjustmentBehavior = .never` (`:69`), so the page handles every inset itself (it does: measured on the Duo); edge-swipe Back/Forward on (`:68`); page events go through `send(_:)` (`:250`).
- `app/ios/README.md:5`: "iPhone only, portrait, iOS 26 and later". The store plan leaves out iPad layouts and landscape (`docs/design/2026-11-24-store-app-draft.md:291`), citing an unverified report that iOS 27.2 devices lose the left/right insets (`:159`).
- Multitasking sizes: as an iPhone-only app it never receives iPad window sizes; on iPad it runs in compatibility mode (one iPhone-sized window, with the iPadOS window controls over its top-left corner — measured). On the Duo, iOS shows it on the inner display at 951×669 regardless of the portrait lock (measured); whether it can also be placed in Split View on the inner display was not observed.

What must change:

1. **iPad**: set `TARGETED_DEVICE_FAMILY = "1,2"` for the app target (and tests); add `UISupportedInterfaceOrientations~ipad` with all four orientations; do not add `UIRequiresFullScreen` (we want windowing and side by side). App Store Connect will then need iPad screenshots, and review happens at iPad sizes — so ship it together with, or after, the size-class rule (otherwise iPad portrait gets one pane at a time).
2. **iPad window controls**: when windowed, keep the page's top-leading control clear of them — constrain the web view's top edge to `view.layoutGuide(for: .safeArea(cornerAdaptation: …))` (`UIView.LayoutRegion`, iPadOS 26) while windowed, or send that region through `send(_:)` as CSS variables the page adds to its top-leading padding. First check in Device Hub whether WebKit already reports it as `safe-area-inset-top`/`-left`.
3. **Fold**: read `view.reservedRegions(kind: .division)` on layout and geometry changes and send the fold (x, width, orientation, in web-view points) to the page through the Bridge — Apple's recommended route for WKWebView, since the page has no API for it and the insets do not include it.
4. **iPhone orientation**: stay portrait-only until the compact-height layout is in; then allow landscape (the Duo already ignores the lock on its inner display). Before that, verify on a real iOS 27.2 device the "insets lost in landscape" report.
5. **`OfflineView.swift:59-60`**: pin leading/trailing to `safeAreaLayoutGuide`.
6. Keep `contentInsetAdjustmentBehavior = .never` (the page already honours the Duo's asymmetric inset — measured) and `UIApplicationSupportsMultipleScenes = false` for the first iPad release.
7. Keep `UIUserInterfaceStyle = Light` until the web app has a dark theme (Part 1 dark-mode rows), then remove it.
8. **Test matrix in Device Hub**: Duo closed, open, half-open (book), rotated, and Split View on the inner display; iPad mini and 13" full screen in both orientations, windowed halves, thirds and quarters, Slide Over; and, until the iPad build ships, the iPhone build on iPad (what App Review sees).

---

## Could not verify

Guidance (Part 1)

- Nothing failed to download. Not read: the guides' `demo.html`, `expectations.md`, task files and the evaluation harness (test material, not guidance).
- 34 guides have no body (listed in Part 1) and were judged on their one-line description only; 8 are drafts in the guidance itself.
- Contrast figures were computed from CSS values, flattening translucent layers over `#f1effd`, white and the gradient stops; spot-check on screen.
- Needs assistive technology or a device: whether VoiceOver/TalkBack announce the toast (mounted with its text) and handle the `aria-modal` div dialogs; where VoiceOver's cursor lands when a focused row is hidden; long-press with VoiceOver and Voice Control; Android back and the iOS edge swipe with a dialog open (the shell turns the swipe on, `ShellViewController.swift:68`); iOS rubber-banding under modals and whether WebKit honours `overscroll-behavior` there; modals under the iOS keyboard; forced-colours (Windows contrast themes) rendering of focus, selected states and brand marks; touch hit areas and tap feedback; the 8 px resize strip on a phone.
- Needs a real browser run: the Esc that cancels a Pinyin candidate (which key event Safari/Chrome deliver) and the provider form's Enter-while-composing; focus loss when the chat list re-sorts; Trusted Types breakage (Report-Only run); whether `style-src 'unsafe-inline'` is still needed; whether iOS 16.4 WebKit applies layout containment for `container-type` (decides whether dialogs must leave the panes first — they should anyway).
- Needs production: live response headers (the hosting edge may add or strip some), the HTTP→HTTPS redirect, whether responses are compressed (the Fastify app does none itself), whether edge logs keep query strings (the WebSocket ticket is in one).
- Needs profiling: the real cost of rebuilding the view model on every streamed delta, on a mid-range phone and in the WKWebView.
- Browser-support details quoted from memory and treated as optional enhancements only: `RequestInit.priority`, `scrollend`, Event Timing, `field-sizing`, `content: … / ""` alternative text. WebMCP is Chromium-flag-only and was not testable.
- Two reviewers wrote that the iOS shell source was not available; it is, on `origin/lane/mac-app-shell`. From it: the shell forces Light appearance (`Info.plist:65`), sets no `pageZoom` and nothing for Dynamic Type (grep), and enables edge-swipe navigation (`ShellViewController.swift:68`).

Devices (Part 2)

- No real device was used. The Duo observations are from the simulator (reported to me); iPad signed in was not observed at all; desktop sizes were reasoned from the CSS, not rendered.
- iPhone Duo inner display: Apple publishes pixels (1878×2670) but no scale; the 951×669 pt size rests on the measurement and WebKit's widths. The fold's exact position and width are not published (assumed centred).
- The Duo outer right inset (~76 pt) is derived from "the status column starts at about 390 pt", not read from `env(safe-area-inset-right)`; the top and bottom insets there were not measured.
- Whether the shell can be put into Split View on the Duo's inner display, and whether it ever shows on the outer display in landscape.
- iPad: the minimum window width and Slide Over width under iPadOS 26.2/27 (not in Apple's pages read); what `pointer`/`hover`/`any-pointer` report on iPad with a trackpad or Magic Keyboard; whether WKWebView reports the iPad window controls as a safe-area inset; the exact use of `UIView.LayoutRegion` corner adaptation for a web view (check in Xcode).
- Whether App Store validation still requires all four iPad orientations for an iPad app that supports multitasking (long-standing rule, recalled from memory, not re-checked).
- The iPhone landscape side-inset values (~59 px a side on Dynamic Island phones) are recalled, not measured.
- The store plan's report that iOS 27.2 devices lose left/right insets in landscape (`docs/design/2026-11-24-store-app-draft.md:159`) — not verified; the simulator honours the right inset on the Duo.
- Apple's iPhone Duo tech-talk videos (linked from the HIG page) were not watched.
- The see-through chat header at 951 pt is reported as measured; its cause (`.chat-head` has no background) is my reading of the CSS, not confirmed in the simulator.
